# Window placement

Window UI, changed or new, follows this file. [canvas-look.md](canvas-look.md) and [spec.md](spec.md) win when they name the same control.

The dated audit [.ui-ergonomics/2026-11-20-main-window.md](../.ui-ergonomics/2026-11-20-main-window.md) is the measurement of the main window, the Run menu, and node search (from source, text size 12). This file is the rule set that measurement produced, and the list a later change keeps current.

## Rules

Apply these to every control this change adds or moves. The procedure for a control the list below does not settle is the `ui-ergonomics` skill (`references/constraints.md`, `motor.md`, `search.md`, `grouping.md`).

- **Floor.** The press target is at least 24px on its smaller side. A smaller target names its exception in the **Decisions** row: the size is essential (a port on the 6u row), or a 24px circle centered on it meets no other target.
- **Primary.** Run on Ctrl+Enter is the main action (spec 12.3). It stays a key on this window, and the Run menu stays the pointer method. A new surface has one primary control, with a treatment the other controls on that surface do not share.
- **Destructive.** Its own chunk. The gap before it is at least twice the gap inside the neighboring chunk. It is not the last row of a menu. A reversible action keeps undo on this window.
- **Gaps.** One gap inside a chunk. The gap between chunks is at least twice that, in `u`. A label sits closer to its own control than to the control before it.
- **Learned place.** A menu order, or a command the spec marks as matching ComfyUI, stays where it is. Size and gap may change. A reorder waits until that row moves from **Not scheduled** to **Open**.
- **Posture.** The canvas is sovereign: keep its density, and raise a frequent target or anything under the floor. A dialog or a popover is transient: fewer decisions, and its primary target may be larger.
- **Same surface.** The steps of Run stay on this window.

## References

Blender and Photoshop are the usability references for the creative surfaces (owner decision, 2026-11-22): their conventions have years of refinement, and people arrive knowing them. A new creative surface (the Canvas view, its tool strip, layers, masks, areas and workspaces) starts from what they do, and the **Decisions** row names the convention it follows and any place it differs and why.

- **Take:** the interaction patterns and keys (a left tool strip, a properties area beside the work, layers with an eye, opacity, blend mode and a mask thumbnail, hover and F2 rename, drag-number fields, area headers with an editor-type menu, workspaces as saved layouts, Q for a quick mask, Space to pan, alt-click the eye to solo).
- **Do better where they are rough:** Blender's learning curve and Photoshop's density. The default stays the simple one-area window (hobbyists); the depth sits behind opt-in workspaces and advanced settings (film teams). Fugue's own rules above still win when they conflict (floor, primary, destructive, gaps).
- **Never copy assets or code:** no icons, artwork, names used as branding, or source. Blender's code is GPL, so only its manual and developer documentation are read; Photoshop is read from Adobe's public documentation.
- **Keys:** adopt their key where the command registry has no owner for it; a collision is settled in the Decisions row, never by silently taking the key.

## On a change or a new surface

1. Read **Rules**, **Open**, and **Not scheduled**, and the canvas-look or spec section for each control you add or move.
2. Each of those controls meets **Rules**. A **Not scheduled** row for that control stays as written.
3. An **Open** row for a control this change touches is finished in this change.
4. Record the control under **Decisions**: file, old value, new value, and the rule it meets.
5. When the decision belongs in the look or the spec, write it there and leave a pointer in the row.

Done when every added or moved control has a **Decisions** row that names the rule it meets, any **Open** row for a control this change touches has moved to **Decisions**, and every **Not scheduled** row is unchanged.

## Cohesion checklist

Before a new surface or control is done, find the long-standing surface that does the same job and check each line against it. The audit these lines come from, with the reference table and the open findings: [.ui-ergonomics/2026-11-22-canvas-areas-cohesion.md](../.ui-ergonomics/2026-11-22-canvas-areas-cohesion.md).

- [ ] **Components.** An existing component is reused: `ViewButton` (the one button; `SmallButton` and the area header's buttons are its variants, D4), `SettingsParts` (`SmallButton`, `Toggle`, `Setting`), `Question`, `KitDialogButtons` (every dialog's actions), `LargeViewDialog`, `WidgetMenu`, `CropFrame`, `CompareView`, `CompareModeButtons`, `KitNotice` (every notice: a tone, a title, lines, actions, copy, dismiss), `ContextBox`, `NewCanvasDialog`, `canvasMenu.js`, `ValueSlider`, `Glyph`, `FugueScrollBar`. A private copy (a button, a mode list, a file dialog, a menu's rows) is a finding; extend the component instead.
- [ ] **Tokens.** No color, font size or radius typed as a literal: colors from `look.*`, sizes in `u`, corners from `look.smallCorner` or `look.popupCorner`, edges 1px `look.hairline`, glyphs 14px. A literal over a picture (white, a dark halo) is the same one `CropFrame` uses.
- [ ] **One primary.** Only the primary is filled with the accent. A toggle or a current choice on that surface uses the quiet marking (`accented: false`), never the accent fill (D7). Never two primaries on screen at once, even in two parts of one surface.
- [ ] **Destructive.** `danger` tint (in a dialog's row the solid red, `dangerSolid` with `dangerInk`, 4.5:1 in every shipped theme), its own chunk (`dialogRole: "destructive"`), last in its row or just before the acting answer when there is one (Cancel, Discard, Save), the gaps around it at least twice the row's gap. A reversible removal is tinted too. A question about a loss highlights the safe answer (Cancel, Keep), and the destructive answer is never the default (D8). A question's answers are `ThemedButton`s (the `ViewButton` alias) in a `KitDialogButtons` row, never a Material `Button` or a row of one's own (#235).
- [ ] **Order.** A dialog's actions use `KitDialogButtons`: one row at the bottom right, GNOME's order (the extras at the far start, Cancel or Back, the destructive answer, the primary last at the right; D9, 2026-11-27). Left and Right move between its buttons, Enter presses the focused one (the default when none is), Escape cancels.
- [ ] **Words.** The same action has the same verb everywhere (Accept, Drop, Export…, OK for a commit that closes); a button is a verb; an ellipsis only when a dialog or file picker follows; sentence case; status words capitalized (Current, Missing, Candidate); Head capitalized as a name; a node by its title, never its id; "Canvas" is the picture, "graph" the node view, in code names too only where a person reads them (D10); **candidate** for a run's pending result, never "result" or "fill" (D3).
- [ ] **Glyphs.** Lucide from the subset (`share/fonts/lucide-fugue.txt`), never a text "×", "▾" or "‹"; a glyph means one thing in the whole window.
- [ ] **States.** Hover, pressed, focus (the 2px accent ring for keyboard focus only, `activeFocus && FocusInput.keyboard`, never after a mouse press; a text field takes no ring, its edge lifts to 1px of `label` at 45%, `KitWell.focused`), disabled (dimmed, with a tip saying why: `ViewButton` shows it on hover even though Qt gives a disabled item no hover, #281) and error (`look.error`) as `ViewButton` draws them; a toggle tells assistive tools it is on.
- [ ] **Keys.** One keymap for a picture and the graph: Home fits, 1 is 100%, + and - zoom, Escape closes or cancels, Enter takes the surface's primary when the focus is not in a text field (D5); a new key is checked against the registry and the References, and a tooltip names it.
- [ ] **Keyboard.** Every control and list row is reached by Tab or the arrows, in reading order.
- [ ] **Right-to-left.** A popup sets `LayoutMirroring` on its content (it is outside the window's mirrored root); a picture is never mirrored.
- [ ] **Empty and error.** An empty state says what to do in the app (a button), not on the command line; the same state has the same words everywhere.
- [ ] **No technical text around the work.** No internal text over or under the work area unless the person needs it to act on: sizes, zoom and positions go in one thin status line (Photoshop's); how-to lines are tooltips; the details a pro wants sit behind an Advanced toggle or a tooltip, never a paragraph (#234).
- [ ] **Pictures.** A `FUGUE_SHOT_DIR` picture of the surface, light and dark, looked at beside the reference surface's; narrow width too for anything an area can host.

The D numbers are the audit's decisions of 2026-11-22. D4 (one button: `ViewButton` with its `bordered`, `flat`, `compact`, `primary`, `danger`, `checkable` variants; `SmallButton` and the area header's `BarButton` are thin aliases) and D5 (one keymap: Home or 0 fits a picture and the Graph, `+` and `-` zoom both, Enter takes the primary; a focused Canvas view keeps the Graph's keys from the Graph) are built (second batch, below); a new button or key extends them, never a copy.

## Open

### 2026-11-23 Share… of the Canvas view's menu (#249)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasFileDialogs.qml`: the question `canvasShareAsk` (`canvasShareWithHistory`, `canvasShareHead`) and the save dialog `canvasShareDialog` (a `FilePicker`); the row `canvas-view.share` (Canvas > Share…, `menus.cpp`, `CanvasView.qml`) in place of the greyed placeholder; the Import picture… dialog (`canvasViewImportDialog`) also takes `*.fuguepack`. The window's File > Open takes one too (`Workflows::openCanvasPackage`).
- Old: Share… was a greyed row ("Not built yet (#249)").
- New: Share… asks one question, what goes in the package (As it is now… keeps no history, With history… keeps every step), each answer opens the portal's save dialog; Cancel closes. The notice (`canvasViewNotice`) says what was saved, or, on Import picture…, what was opened, with its history when it has one, and when its id was taken so it got a new one.
- Why: sharing is a choice with a real consequence (the history is bigger and shows every step), so it is asked once, at the moment of the share, with the smaller, more private answer as the default (Hick: two answers). A canvas never replaces another on opening, so the person has nothing to confirm there, only to be told.
- Rules met: Floor (answer buttons 28px), Primary (As it is now… the one accent fill, highlighted and focused, as D8 does for the safe answer), Destructive (nothing is removed; the package never overwrites a file), D9 (the primary answer last, at the right), Words (Share…, With history…, As it is now…, never "head" or a hash), Right-to-left (the row mirrors).

### 2026-11-27 Compact node cards: an output and an input on one row, a category dot, one picture corner (#476, follow-ups of #466)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasNode.qml` (the output and input rows, the header's dot `kind`, `pictureCorner`), `canvas_model.cpp` (`Port::sharesRow`, the `category` role), `CanvasLook::categoryColor`, `CompareView.qml` / `CropView.qml` (`cornerRadius`, `viewCorners`, `shaders/corners.frag`), `MediaView.qml` (`corner`), `MaskNodeView.qml`.
- Old: one row per port (a picture-in, picture-out node had two rows for one line of the board); the header's dot was the first output's port mark; the Mask, Crop, Compare and Media viewers on a card kept the Flat thumbnail corner.
- New: in Compact an output and a socket-only input (no widget, section or `shown_when`) share a row, the input left and the output right, so such a node is a row shorter; the header dot is the node type's top-level category in a colour that reads on the card (a pack's node with no known category keeps the kind mark); every viewer on the face has the Compact thumbnail's corner. Flat is unchanged.
- Why: the board's Compact card puts the two on one line and colours the dot by category; the mockup's picture corner is one recipe for every picture. Shared rows only pair a plain socket so a row never gains a control or vanishes when a value is linked (the row would jump).
- Rules met: Floor (rows stay 26px; the whole shared row is the targets of two ports: the input's dot at the left edge and the output's at the right edge, each 10px and the same as before, the hover help follows the pointer's half), Primary (none), Destructive (none), Contrast (the dot 3:1 on the card by construction, and the title carries the meaning; labels as before), Gaps (a label is capped at half the card, so two labels never touch), Keys (none changed).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm; confidence medium-high): pairing is by order (the k-th output with the k-th plain socket input), not by type, so a node with two outputs and one socket-only input shares only the first row; the category map is a table in `CanvasLook::categoryColor` over the six top-level categories the built-in nodes use; Video takes dusk's rose because five tones serve six categories.
- Not checked by a person: the Crop, Compare and Media corners on a real GPU with a picture in them (the corner shader is one item with no texture; a Load Image card, the rows, the dots and the wires at 100% and 40% were seen in a hidden-session look-shot). Group faces, the magnifier and the live Sampler frame in Compact were not looked at again.

### 2026-11-27 Model picker: a file the engine does not recognise, and the engine's family (#703)

- Control: the model menu of a model input (`src/canvas/widgets/InputWidget.qml`) and of the App view (`src/apps/AppModelInput.qml`); the row's `engineNote` and `family` come from `modelRow` (`src/workflow/model_picker.cpp`).
- Old: a diffusion model or checkpoint of a family Fugue's table does not know sat under its folder name with no word on it, whether or not the engine would load it.
- New: with a local helper, such a file is grouped under the engine's family name ("Qwen Image"), and one the engine does not recognise has the second line "stable-diffusion.cpp does not recognise this model" (the row's note, where a fit note or the Recommended reason goes: the fit note wins, then this line, then the reason). No new control, chip or colour.
- Why: the second line is where a row already says why it is not the obvious pick; a label on the row keeps the file pickable (a paired host may run another engine), so it warns and never blocks. The answer arrives after the list opens (it is asked on the header worker), as the Recommended mark does.
- Rules met: Floor (no new control), Primary (no new accent: the line is plain note text), Learned place (the row's note).
- Not checked by a person: the line in a window (a model test proves the row).

### Port loses to the resize corner

- File: `src/canvas/FugueCanvas.qml` (`cornerAt` runs before `portUnder`)
- Rule: canvas-look §4 and spec Appendix A. A port inside the corner square starts a wire.
- Acceptance: at zoom 1, a left press at a port center that lies inside the corner square starts a link and does not call `beginResize`. A press in that square with no port still resizes.

## Not scheduled

Drafts from the 2026-11-20 pass that the rules above already answered. A window change leaves these as they are until the owner moves the row to **Open**.

| Control | As it is | Why it stays |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Delete on the selection toolbar | 2px from the neighboring buttons | An 8px gap is below what a person notices |
| Run-error row | height is the line plus 8px (22 for one line at text size 12) | The floor would make one line 24 |
| Run menu title | the same treatment as the other titles | Spec 12.3: the main action is Ctrl+Enter |
| Slider click | opens the editor; a drag sets the value | The same click as the other wells |
| Delete in the node menu | last row of `menus.cpp` `node()` | Spec 12.4 keeps the ComfyUI order |
| Resize corner | 4u, and at least 12 screen px | The size written in canvas-look §4 |
| Selection toolbar | one row of glyphs | canvas-look §11, the ComfyUI toolbox |

## Decisions

### 2026-11-27 One row of actions: every dialog's buttons in `KitDialogButtons`, GNOME's order, at the bottom right (owner report; coordinator's decision, confidence medium-high, the owner's to overturn)

- Owner (2026-11-27): "Dialogs inconsistencies regarding the placement of 'Cancel', 'Discard' style red buttons... those should always end up in the bottom right corner... and it would be nice if keyboard navigation was also supported to change button choice and hit enter to pick a button."
- Control: `src/kit/KitDialogButtons.qml` (new) and every dialog with actions: the questions (`quitAsk`, `closeAsk`, `closeAllAsk`, `trustAsk`, `recoverAsk`, `remoteConsentAsk`, `deleteGroupQuestion`, `downloadAsk`, `providerRemoveAsk`, `canvasTaskClearAsk`, the Canvas view's `canvasPropertiesAsk`, `canvasRenameAsk`, `canvasShareAsk`, `canvasClearAsk`, `canvasDeleteAsk`, `canvasModifyAsk`, `canvasTransformAsk`, the Graph's `groupSinksAsk`, `groupUpdateAsk`, `libraryRenameAsk`, `renameAsk`, `groupInterfaceEditor`), the forms (`newCanvasDialog`, `canvasExportDialog`, `canvasBatchDialog`, `appBuilderDialog`), the sheets (`templateDetails`, `getReadySheet`, `modelSetupSheet`), the Projects dialog's New, Project settings and Unsaved work views, the Packs and Cache pages' questions (`packQuestion`, `cacheQuestion`), and the footers of Crop, Region and the mask editor. `QuestionAnswers.qml` is gone.
- Old: one row per dialog, made by hand: the close-tab question put Discard alone at the far left; the Packs question put Yes before No at the left of its card; the Cache question sat at the left; Projects used a 1u gap; Crop and Region had Cancel and OK just after their fields, at the left; App builder's Remove app sat at the far left; Batch put Cancel batch before Hide; Enter, Escape and the arrows did what each dialog had wired, or nothing.
- New: the row lays its buttons out by `dialogRole`, whatever their order in the file: `extra` (Read the license, Download the template's own files…) at the far start apart, a stretch, `cancel`, `destructive` (its own chunk, 4u before and, when an acting answer follows, after), `accept` (the default role; the primary last). Right to left it mirrors. Keys: a `Question` that opens with nothing of its own focused gives the focus to the default answer (`defaultButton`, else the visible `primary` one), and its answers forget their focus when it closes (a focus scope would reopen on the answer left last, a Discard); Left and Right move the focus between the row's buttons as seen (right to left too) and stop at the ends; Tab and Shift+Tab walk them in the same order; Enter or Space press the focused button, with the keyboard's ring; in a `Question`, Enter where no button and no text field has the focus presses the default, and Escape anywhere in it presses the `cancel` answer (a question with none closes as its `closePolicy` says, so Recover, which has no cancel, still waits). A text field keeps its own Enter. In a dialog's row the focus ring of the accent-filled primary and of the solid red answer is drawn 3px outside it, 2px of accent (of the error colour on the red), as GNOME's focus outline, by the row (one item, put inside the focused answer): an accent ring on the accent did not show, and an ink ring inside read as a smaller button on a light card (review, 2026-11-27); other buttons keep the 2px ring on their edge, and a primary outside a dialog row the fill's ink (`accentForeground`), as `KitChip`'s.
- Order and default per dialog (after): Quit: Keep working (cancel, default), Quit (destructive). Close tab: Cancel, Discard (destructive), Save (default). Close all tabs: Cancel (default), Discard and close all (destructive). Trust: Not now (cancel, highlighted, default and focused), Allow: Allow lets a pack's programs run as you, so it takes a deliberate press (D8; review, 2026-11-27). Recover: Discard (destructive), Recover (default). Remote consent: No, cancel the run (cancel, highlighted, default and focused), Yes, send it (D8). Delete group, Remove provider, Clear prompt history, Clear canvas, Delete canvas, Cache: the safe answer (cancel, default), the removal (destructive). Model download: Read the license (extra), Cancel, Download (default: nothing is lost, and a stop keeps the part and resumes, 2026-11-27). Layer properties, Rename canvas, Expand/Shrink selection, Transform selection, Rename port, New canvas, Export: Cancel, the act (default). Group update: Cancel, Update (default), but Cancel is the highlighted default when the update takes inputs or outputs away, since their values and links go on every instance (D8; a newer major keeps them and undo moves it back, so Move stays the default). Share: Cancel, With history…, As it is now… (default). Group sinks: Cancel, Put inside, Leave outside (default). Library rename: Only here (cancel, highlighted default), Rename in the library: it rewrites the library's folder and id, and no undo brings that back (D8). Group editor: Done (default). App builder: Cancel, Remove app (destructive, red, editing only), Apply (the default: Enter from a field, a toggle or a row applies and stays, as a field's Enter did), Apply and open App view (the primary). Batch: before a run Close (cancel), Run (default; while Run cannot act the focus goes to Close: a question whose default cannot act focuses its cancel answer); running Hide (cancel), Cancel batch (destructive); finished New batch (extra, at the start), Close (cancel, the primary and default, last). Template details: Download the template's own files… (extra), Cancel, Open (default). Get ready: Cancel or Stop, or Close (cancel), Try again or Resume, Install (default). Model folders: Skip (cancel), the primary; on the consent screen and while it looks, Skip has the focus, so Enter skips and never starts a search by habit; once there is something to say yes to, the focus moves to the primary (the sheet's own keys). Projects New: Back (cancel), Create, Create and open (default); Project settings: Back or Close (cancel), Save (default); Unsaved work: Cancel, Open in new window (default). Packs: No (cancel), Yes or Run it (default); a removal: No (default), Remove (destructive). Crop, Region, mask editor: the fields or the hint at the start, Cancel, OK (default).
- Why GNOME's order: the owner asked for the red and cancelling buttons at the bottom right; GNOME's HIG puts the cancel button first ("Always ensure that the cancel button appears first, before the affirmative button. In left-to-right locales, this is on the left") and libadwaita's own save question is Cancel, Discard (destructive), Save (suggested, the default response) (`AdwAlertDialog`, its GtkBuildable example); macOS agrees ("Always place the default button on the trailing side of a row... Cancel buttons are typically on the leading side", Apple HIG, Alerts). KDE (and Windows) put Cancel last (Qt's `QDialogButtonBox::KdeLayout`: OK, Apply, Cancel); Fugue already followed D9 (Cancel first, the primary last at the right) in most dialogs, runs on Omarchy beside GNOME-ordered apps, and the reference creative tools on macOS use the trailing default, so GNOME's order changes the fewest dialogs and matches what people meet most. GNOME and Apple go further for a question about a loss and set no default at all ("this should not be done if its action is irreversible, destructive"; "you don't want to make a Cancel button the default"); Fugue keeps D8 (the safe answer highlighted and focused, so a stray Enter keeps everything), which is as safe and needs no decision from the person.
- Decisions taken without the owner, each with its confidence: the destructive answer sits between Cancel and the acting answer when both exist, as libadwaita's save question (high); App builder's Remove app moves from the far start into that place in the danger tint, since undo brings the app back (medium); a Packs removal is red and No becomes the highlighted answer, by D8 (medium-high); Trust and remote consent highlight and default to the safe answer, Not now and No, since their yes runs a pack's programs or sends data off the machine (high; review); the Projects views keep Escape for closing the whole dialog, so their row does not take Escape (`cancelOnEscape: false`; medium-high); the inline confirmations on a row (Projects' Remove from list, the Packs library's Remove) and Get ready's per-row Skip and Allow stay on their row, apart from the dialog's actions, because they answer for that row (high); `KitNotice` actions (the CUDA helper's Copy, Get ready…, Undo) are a notice's, not a dialog's, and stay in its head (high); the LargeViewDialog footer is shown whenever a footer is given (a hidden footer measured 0 and never came back once the Region window's info line hid it) (high).
- 2026-11-27 (owner, second look): one button size in every dialog row, 7u tall, 3u each side, the text size in Medium, for the questions' `ViewButton`s and the sheets' `KitChip`s alike (before: the chips 5u wider and 13/12 of the text size, the questions' buttons the label size); and the destructive answer is the theme's red as a solid fill in light and dark themes alike (a red that reads as red apart from the accent: `red`, else `bright_red`, else a fixed red; review 2026-11-27: hackerman's green, vantablack's and solitude's grey and white's black "red" lost the meaning) (before: in light themes a pale pink tint), with the ink that reads on it (white, or the theme's dark colour on a pastel red) at 4.5:1; the red moves only when neither ink reaches it (canvas-look 6.1, `danger-solid`; 18 of the 23 shipped themes keep their red, the label 4.55 to 14.35). On an accent or solid red fill the raise's soft shadow (`KitRaise.under`: a KitChip primary, the App strip's Run) is drawn under the fill, not over it: over it, it made Get ready's Install read as disabled in a dark theme. Other raised chips keep it over their fill: their colours were tuned with it, and without it a light theme's chips melt into their group (pictured, 2026-11-27). Toolbar and row buttons keep their size and tint.
- Rules met: Order (D9 for every dialog, one component), Destructive (its own chunk, 4u, never the default), Primary (one per row), Keyboard (arrows, Tab, Enter, Escape), Right-to-left (mirrors with the content; the arrows go as seen), Floor (the buttons' own 7u).
- Checked by `test_kit_dialog_buttons` (order, gaps, arrows, Tab, Enter on the focused and on no button, Escape, right to left) and `test_question_dialogs` (the close-tab order). Pictures: `test_dialog_shots` (FUGUE_SHOT_DIR, dark, light and right to left), every dialog above.

### 2026-11-27 Typed numbers: one rule for every number typed into a field (review of the group editor; coordinator's decision, confidence medium-high, the owner's to overturn)

- Control: `src/typed_number.h` (`TypedNumber`, a QML singleton: `pattern` for a `RegularExpressionValidator`, which changes with the language (`patternChanged`, sent by `Translator::reload` after `QLocale::setDefault`), `parse`, `normalize`; `TypedNumber::read` for C++), used by the group editor's range fields (`exposedMin<i>`, `exposedMax<i>`; `GroupEdit::editExposedInput` refuses a limit that is not a number with a problem instead of clearing it), the App builder's (`builderMin-<i>`, `builderMax-<i>`), a node's number widgets (`WidgetEdit::parse`, and `FieldEditor` while Up and Down step), a slider's typed value (`KitSlider.typed`) and the Canvas view's Modify and Transform selection fields (`CanvasSelectionDialogs`).
- Old: each site had its own rule. Most read every comma as a decimal point (`replace(",", ".")`), so "1,000" was stored as 1 for an English reader; `DoubleValidator` took a thousands separator and refused an empty field (a range could not be cleared).
- New: digits, an optional sign, a decimal point, an optional exponent. The point is always a decimal point. A comma is one only where the language writes the decimal point as a comma (`QLocale::decimalPoint()`, Qt.locale()); elsewhere the validator refuses it, so nothing ambiguous is stored. A thousands separator is never accepted. What is stored is written with a point, as `String()` shows a number in every language. A range field also takes empty (no limit). A number a double cannot hold ("1e999", "1e-400") is refused. The Transform fields keep their limits (Scale 1 to 1000 %, Rotate -360 to 360, Move -99999 to 99999, one decimal): a field out of range or half-typed shows its text in the error colour and its range in a tooltip, and Apply and Enter wait (never a silent clamp). Numbers in a workflow file are JSON numbers, not typed text, so saved workflows load as before.
- Follow-ups (#690, 2026-11-27, no new control): the group editor's Default field for a number input is read by the same rule (`GroupEdit::editExposedInput`, `TypedNumber::read` on the language's locale; a Danish "0,5" is 0.5). The Canvas view's Modify and Transform fields follow a live language switch: `inRange` reads `TypedNumber.pattern`, so `ready` and `transformReady` re-evaluate when the language changes. Decided and not done, medium-high confidence: (a) a Danish thousand typed with a point ("1.024") still reads as 1.024: it is a valid decimal in a float widget, a stricter rule would need to know which widgets take whole numbers, and the old reading was the same; the engine refuses a non-whole value for a whole-number input with a reason, so nothing wrong is stored silently; (b) seeds above 2^53 still go through a double: Fugue's seed range ends at 2^53, so nothing real is lost. The rule follows Fugue's UI language, not the system number format (a Danish user running Fugue in English types "0.5"); the error message says so, and the next release notes should too.
- Checked by `test_typed_number` ("1,000" refused and "0.5" taken in English; "0,5" is 0.5 and "-0,5" is -0.5 in Danish; "1e999" and "1e-400" refused; partial numbers are intermediate; the singleton follows `QLocale::setDefault` and says its pattern changed), `test_widget_edit` (`typedNumbersAreCheckedByTheEngineAndOneStepEach`, English and Danish), `test_group_edit` (`aRangeLimitThatIsNotANumberIsRefused`).

### 2026-11-27 Alt+Q shows the Queue where it was hidden (owner bug report; medium-high confidence)

- Control: `panel.queue` (Alt+Q), `Workspace::toggleQueue` (`src/workspace/workspace.cpp`). No new control.
- Bug: hiding the Queue closed its area and forgot it, so showing it again always made the default bottom split, even when it had been side by side.
- Decision: closing the Queue remembers its place: the axis, which side of its neighbour, its share of the two areas' room together, and the neighbour's area id (the first area of the neighbour when that is a split). Showing it splits that area the same way. When the area is gone, or the result would not fit the window, it opens at the default (30% below the active area). Never shown means the default. One memory per window, kept as `queuePlace` in the layout file while the layout is not the default; a hidden Queue that leaves the default one-area window has no file, so that memory lasts for the session only. Reset layout forgets it.
- Convention: Blender restores a maximized area to where it was (Ctrl+Space toggles), but closing an area (Join, Close) keeps nothing; the Queue here toggles like the maximize, so it restores like it (read from Blender's manual in memory, not re-fetched; nothing copied). Differs: Blender has no one key that opens an area of a given type.
- Confidence: medium-high that the file is the right home (it is the layout's own, and a stale id is checked on use).

### 2026-11-27 Follow-ups of the dialog buttons (#696) and the notices (#681) (coordinator's decisions, confidence medium-high, the owner's to overturn)

- Control: `src/look.cpp` (`dangerFill`, `dangerHover`, `kitChip`), `src/apps/AppBuilderDialog.qml` (the preview column, `builderPreviewColumn`), `src/kit/KitNotice.qml` (the queued announcement), `src/workflow/workflows.cpp` and `model_choice.h` (the notice's tab and reading line), `AppWindow.qml` and `WorkflowDialogs.qml` (`announceChanges`), `TemplateDetails.qml` (`yourFiles`).
- Dialog buttons: the danger tint of the chips outside dialog rows (Projects' Remove from list…, Packs' Cancel while working) is mixed from `dangerSolid`, the dialog answer's red, not the theme's raw `red`: hackerman's chips were green. App builder: the preview column shows only when the dialog is 150u wide or more (600 px); below that the inputs list takes the whole width, so a row's remove button and "Also set…" are whole at 560 px (before, the list had 60% of the dialog and the row's controls were cut). The preview is a picture of the form, so it is the part given up, not a control. The App pictures' tooltip: the capture moves the pointer out of the window first.
- Notices: a stored swap banner is dropped when another line shows on return (it came back on a much later visit before). The banner's Undo is the latest swap's only (`ModelChoice` keeps one record): a tab's banner comes back without Undo after a swap on another tab. Decision (medium-high): stated here, not a record per tab, because Undo puts back inputs that later edits and swaps may have changed, so an older record is the one most likely to undo the wrong thing; the banner still says what was changed. The end of "Reading your file…" sets the line once, so the card is not built twice and a focused button keeps the focus. A change of words is announced once the reading line is gone, keyed on the line itself (`readingNotice`), not on every read (Get ready's reads have no line). The details and the banner count your files by path. The tier badge's label on `kitChip` now reads at 4.5:1 in every shipped theme (everforest was 4.29 and gruvbox 4.45: the chip is lessened toward the group until it does; the chip's step from its group is a little smaller there). The announcement queued by a notice is skipped once the notice is gone.
- Rules met: Tokens (the red from the theme's tokens, `u` for the 150u), Gaps and Floor (unchanged), Words (unchanged), screen readers (announced once).

### 2026-11-27 One notice component, five tones; the swap banner says each file in a line (owner report; confidence medium-high)

- Control: `src/kit/KitNotice.qml` (new; docs/canvas-look.md 20.23) in place of `src/canvas/Banner.qml` (removed): the workflow notice (`workflowNotice`, WorkflowDialogs.qml, its banner block only), the app window's (`appNotice`, AppWindow.qml), the CUDA helper offer (`helperNotice`, Main.qml), the keymap's problems (`keymapNotice`, CommandUi.qml), the Canvas view's toast (`canvasViewHeadToast`) and canvas mismatch (`canvasViewCanvasMismatch`), the layers panel's view states (`canvasLayersSolo`, `canvasLayersRegion`); `ModelChoice` hands the banner a `SwapNotice` (title, one line per file, the whole text) through `Workflows.noticeTitle` and `noticeLines`.
- Old: every notice was one grey paragraph on a slate or moss `Banner` card. Two swaps of "Use my file" read as one run-on paragraph ("Opened with your file X in place of Y (same model, Q8_0, from another source). Opened with your file ..."), easy to miss and hard to scan (owner: "should that note at the bottom not be in some sort of yellow tinted color ... It also look a little weird").
- New: `KitNotice` has a tone (info, attention, error, success, neutral), each the theme's role tint and edge with a 14px tone glyph, all from the Omarchy palette tokens (light and dark); a short title; a body or lines; actions, copy and ×. The swap banner is the **attention** tone, titled "Opened with your file" or "Opened with your 2 files" (or "Using your file", "Using your 2 files" for a tab that took them), one line per model: your file, "in place of", the template's file, the tier as a quiet chip (the title counts your files, so one file standing in for two models is "your file") ("same model · Q8_0 · another source"); names cut in the middle, the whole name (and your file's path) in a tooltip; Undo undoes them all as before; the copy button copies every sentence, one a line. The banner belongs to its tab: another tab shows its own line, and back on the tab the banner and its Undo come back (unless another line took its place). It is announced once, not at every percent of the "Reading your file…" line before it. Get ready offers and the helper offer are info; the keymap's problems and the canvas mismatch attention; a run's layer and the layers panel's view states neutral (compact).
- Why attention, not info, for a swap (medium-high): Fugue changed the workflow's models on its own (the default since #629), the files differ (precision, source), so results and memory use can differ from the template's sample, and Undo holds only while nothing else changed; info is for news that asks nothing. The owner leaned the same way.
- Not moved, and why: the group notice (`groupNotice`, GroupDialogs.qml: another change owns that file now; a straight swap later); the app builder's picking pill (`appPickBanner`: a mode bar with its Done, not a notice); the Needs panel (spec 12.17: a standing checklist, "not a toast"); the Get ready sheet's message and pack warning (a line by the answers of a modal sheet whose rows show the state; its stop form was settled the same day); the error lines of dialogs and the Canvas view's notes (an inline error belongs by the field or picture it is about); the node card's chips and the run pill's errors (the canvas's own look, section 4.7). A workflow notice that is an error still shows neutral: `Workflows::setNotice` has 30 callers with plain text; giving each a tone is its own change.
- Convention: GNOME HIG and libadwaita split by lifetime: a toast for an event, short, dismissible, at most one button, Undo the canonical case (`AdwToast`, developer.gnome.org/hig/patterns/feedback/toasts.html); a banner for a persistent state until it changes, one title and one button (`AdwBanner`, .../banners.html). Fugue follows the split in behaviour (the swap and the run's layer are events with Undo; the keymap, canvas mismatch and view states last while their state does) but uses one component for both. Deviations: libadwaita banners and toasts carry no severity colour; Fugue tones them, because a person scanning the foot of a busy graph needs to tell an offer from a change Fugue made (the owner's report); the notice sits at the foot of the work area as it did (toasts' place), not centred, so a long line has room. Blender's operator reports (info, warning, error in the status bar and the Info editor), ComfyUI's toasts and Photoshop's Contextual Task Bar were the other references named: their pages could not be read for this change (docs.blender.org and helpx.adobe.com refused the fetch, docs.comfy.org does not document its toasts), so nothing here is claimed from them.
- Rules met: Floor (Undo, Get ready…, the × and copy are the existing 7u and 6u buttons), Primary (Get ready… stays the one accent; Undo is raised), Tokens (role tint, edge and ink, `u` sizes, the group corner), Glyphs (Lucide `info`, `triangle-alert`, `circle-x`, `check`, `x`, one meaning each), Keyboard (Tab reaches the buttons in reading order; Escape inside the card takes the key before the window's Escape shortcuts, so a run is not cancelled, dismisses it and gives the focus back to the graph, or the nearest panel when the graph is hidden: no trap), Right-to-left (the card mirrors with the application), screen readers (an alert, announced when it shows). Pictures: `~/.cache/fugue-notice-shots/{before,after}`.

The primary counts your files, not the models they stand in for. The primary says what it does, "Open with your file" or "Open with your 2 files". The download names what it fetches, "Download the template's own files (8.4 GB)…", with a tooltip, and sits alone at the row's start, apart from Cancel and Open.

### 2026-11-27 A stopped download keeps its part; the row can delete it (owner decision; confidence high)

- Control: Get ready's model row (`GetReadyRow.qml`, `GetReadySheet.qml`) and its message line.
- Old: Stop deleted the `.part`; the stop message was long error-red text saying a download "keeps what it had", which was false.
- New: a row with a kept part (after a Stop, a failure or an earlier session) says **Paused** where a row that never started says "Not done", and its size reads "178 MB of 850 MB" (the size so far, and the full size when it is known). Its **Delete** is a trash button on the row itself (`RowTrashButton`, 7u, between the size and the chevron; tooltip and screen-reader name "Delete the partial download (178 MB)"), hidden while that download runs; it removes the part (a regular file only, never through a link) and the row goes back to "Not done". The button takes its own press (`ReleaseWithinBounds`), so it never toggles the row. The headline counts what is left ("Fugue will download 2 models, 967 MB left."), and the finished sheet's retry button says **Resume** while a kept part is left, **Try again** otherwise. The stop message is a plain note in the text colour, no copy button and no "Not done" list (the rows show that): "Stopped. Downloads keep what they had." Install and setup sentences appear only when one was running. A real failure stays red.
- Convention: Steam and download managers pause and resume, with a separate remove; the Hugging Face CLI resumes a partial file. The delete sits on the row's face (owner, 2026-11-27: "I had to manually expand the models to see it"), as an icon next to the status, away from the primary at the foot; it is the only one that touches a file, and the partial is the person's to throw away. It is a separate Tab stop and a separate target, so the row's own tap still opens it.
- Rules met: Destructive (a small icon on the row, never next to the primary; it names the size it throws away; the part is only what the person stopped), Words ("Paused", "Resume", "Delete the partial download (178 MB)"), Keyboard (Tab reaches the button, Enter or Space presses it), Floor (7u, 28px), Gestures (`ReleaseWithinBounds`, #660), Right-to-left (unchanged).

The editor is a dusk `KitHero` ("Group inputs and outputs", and one line: "Choose what this group shows on its card: …"), the name as a `KitRow` (its id and version in the field's tooltip), then Inputs and Outputs as `KitSection`s with an **Add an input…** / **Add an output…** button (a plus, a menu of the inner ports not exposed yet, typing filters a long one) and `KitGroup` rows: the label on the card (placeholder: what the card shows without one, the inner port's label, as `groupFace` gives it: "Model", not the name "model"), Details, Move up, Move down and Stop exposing (flat 6u `ViewButton`s), and under it what the row does in words ("Sets Model on Encode and Sample", "From Image on Picture") with **Also set…** (a plus, flat) where another inner input of the type exists. Details (shut on each opening; its tooltip names what it holds) hold the name links and `--set` use, the section, the default, the range (empty clears a limit; a typed number, see "Typed numbers"), the choices and the type id. An output's label is the shown group's face label (#72, `relabelExposedOutput`: never the inner port's own, which for a nested group instance would relabel that group's definition), its name under Details. An empty list says both ways to add one. The dialog is as tall as its content, up to the window less its margins or 720px; it opens centred, then its top edge stays put, so it grows downwards (upwards only to stay in the window) and the row under the pointer does not jump. Keys: the name has the focus on opening; Tab walks it in reading order; an edit keeps the focus on the control Tab moved to (by its objectName) and a move keeps it on the moved row's; a row's button or Alt+Up and Alt+Down first keep the text typed in the focused field when the field would keep it on its own (`acceptableInput`: a half-typed "-" in a range is left), one step for the text, one for the action, and the action then finds its row by place, so a Name just typed under Details is the name it uses (a button takes no focus); removing a row gives the focus to the row that takes its place, else the one above, else the section's Add button; Up and Down in a label go to the row above or below, Alt+Up and Alt+Down move the row (as the app builder and ComfyUI); Enter keeps a field; Escape closes. The tab rename popup's field is unframed: the popup card is the one frame, the text 3u in, at least 40px tall. A disabled field dims once (50%, as `AppField`); the placeholder is Qt's `PlaceholderText`, which mirrors once in right to left. The App builder's range fields take the same empty-or-number rule.

None is in the Get ready files. `AppField` (the App form's) stays its own component with the same look numbers but 2u in.

Fugue keeps the label, what it sets and the order in each row (the simple path) and folds the rest per row (Details) rather than one selection panel, so a row's settings sit beside it; it adds the menus Blender's plus and ComfyUI's empty slot stand for, so the keyboard needs no canvas. Differs: no socket description (a group input has none in 5.4), no panels other than sections.

- Old: a node widget's list was exactly as wide as the field on screen (at least 120px): on a narrow node, or zoomed out (the field is drawn at the zoom, the list is not), every entry was cut at its right end, a model file name losing the part that tells two files apart (Q4_K_M, fp8, .gguf); the scroll bar's gutter was also taken off each row twice. Each other site had its own width rule (a fixed 30u to 80u, `ThemedDropDown`'s own measure, the area menu's patch after #647).
- New: one rule in `WidgetMenu`: at least the field (`anchorWidth`, in window pixels), as wide as its widest entry needs (text, place badge, detail, insets, the scroll bar's gutter when it scrolls), never less than 32u, at most 160u and the window less its margins. The list is in the window's overlay, so it reads at screen scale at any canvas zoom. A row still too long is cut in the middle in the model menus (`elide: Text.ElideMiddle`, the end of the file name kept), at the right elsewhere, and shows its whole text in a tooltip on hover. The closed model field is cut in the middle too, as a file or folder already was, and when a value is cut the row's tooltip adds it whole under the label and description. Type to filter is unchanged (model menus and lists over 12 rows, #84).

Blender's menus search by typing (4.0). Differs: none of them caps the width; 160u (640px at text size 12) keeps a line readable, and the cut row's tooltip and the middle cut cover what does not fit. ComfyUI's option to scale the list with the zoom is not offered: at the zoom where a node's widgets show (50% and up) the screen-scale list is the readable one.

### 2026-11-27 A size known before a run shows in the header's meta, quieter than a measured one (#601; confidence medium-high)

- Control: the header meta caption of `CanvasNode.qml` (`predictedSize` role, from `CanvasRuns::onChecked` / `CanvasModel::setRunState`).
- Old: a picture's size ("1024 × 1024") appeared only in the thumbnail's info chip, once a run (or, for Load Image, the picked file) had made a picture.
- New: while a node has no measured size, the caption says the size the dry check worked out from the picture upstream (Load Image's header, Resize, Resize to Megapixels, Crop, Pad, Rotate, Flip, Blur, Sharpen, Invert, Brightness and Contrast, Upscale Image, Solid Mask, the latent nodes with a width and height): the label colour, in italics, caption size, the place the time of a run takes. It never shows a guess: an unknown size shows nothing. A node with a result keeps its measure in the chip, and its time in the caption; a stale node whose last result is another size shows the new one, and the caption gives way to the title when they do not both fit.
- Why the header and not the chip: a latent or a mask has no picture slot; the caption is the one place a node with a picture or without has, it adds no item (a group's card gets no size), and it sits where the state is already read. The italics and the missing black chip are what make it "not measured".
- Rules met: Floor (not a control: no press target), Gaps and Learned place (nothing moved: the caption's slot is the existing one), Gestalt (the size sits with the state it describes). Out-of-range sizes are the node's error, shown as #600's are.

- Follow-ups (#671, 2026-11-27; no new control, the caption keeps its rules): (1) the check reports the real size of a cached or frozen node's picture, not its kernel's prediction (`shownSizes` in `scheduler.cpp`); (2) the header's fit test adds up the real implicit widths of the warnings badge, the pin and the state glyph (`meta.otherWidth`) in place of 14 px per glyph; (3) a picture that is only passed on (Blur, Sharpen, Invert, Flip, Rotate, Crop, Brightness and Contrast) keeps its size past 16384, so a 20000 px file shows its size down the chain; the node that makes a new picture that big (Resize, Upscale, Megapixels, Pad) still has no size to show and says so in its own check. Decided, medium-high: the memo of Load Image's header is keyed on `stat` (device, inode, size, mtime and ctime in ns) and the file is opened once with `O_NONBLOCK`, judged by `fstat` and read through the descriptor (`vips_source_new_from_descriptor`), which closes the isFile/open race and a stale same-size, same-millisecond entry.

### 2026-11-26 A click acts on the one control under it, never on what lies beneath (owner report; confidence high)

- Control: `KitCard` (the shelf's app cards, the Templates window's cards, the Apps dialog's), the Templates window's category tabs, and every other `TapHandler` of the window (`bin/check-quick` now fails one with no `gesturePolicy`).
- Old: these handlers used Qt's default, DragThreshold, which takes only a passive grab; Qt then hands the same press to the handlers under them, a modal popup notwithstanding. On a window just started, a click on a Templates card opened that template and the shelf's app under it too ("it also opens the app that happens to be behind the template tile"); a category tab over a shelf card opened that app.

Rows that are also dragged (the Layers panels' rows and mask thumbnails, the Node library's rows, a paint layer's name) keep DragThreshold, named, so the row's DragHandler and the row's own tap still share their press.
- Why: a modal window must take every click on it; the press reaching the window behind made the wrong template or app open in a tab.
- Rules met: no control moved or resized; Floor, Gaps and Keyboard unchanged.

- Why: a panel floating over the Graph hid the work and could not exist while the Graph was hidden (#647); as an area it has a size the person sets by the border, sits where the work is not, and follows the one model every other view follows. Alt+Q stays the quick way, and the toolbar's button its pointer twin.
- Convention: Blender's Timeline and Info editors, areas split off the bottom of the screen and closed again, switched in the editor-type menu. Differs: Blender has no one key that splits one off and closes it; Alt+Q does both, as the panel's key did.
- Rules met: Floor (the rows' buttons are `ViewButton`s, 28px, unchanged), Primary (none: the rows are equal), Destructive (`queueCancel-<run>` keeps its `danger` tint and a chunk of its own at the row's end; closing the area loses nothing, the queue is the files'), Gaps and Words (unchanged; one new word, **Queue**, the Run queue menu row's name), Keyboard (Tab and the arrows move along the buttons as before; Escape no longer closes it, Alt+Q or the area's close button does), States (no change), Right-to-left (the area mirrors with the window), Empty ("Nothing is running." and "Finished runs show here." unchanged).

### 2026-11-26 Any area can switch its view, the Graph's too (#647; decided by the coordinator, medium-high)

- Control: `src/workspace/AreaHeader.qml`, the view-type menu (`areaType-<id>`, the Graph's header in the one-area window too); the commands `area.view-graph` and `area.view-canvas`; `src/Main.qml` (the Graph hidden while no area shows it).
- Old: the Graph's area could not switch away (Canvas and App greyed with the note "the Graph keeps an area"), and closing it gave the Graph to the area that took its place.
- New: every view is choosable in every area. Choosing Graph or App where another area shows it still moves it (the two areas trade, note "moves here"). With a Canvas in the Graph's area the Graph is hidden but alive: its run toolbar, queue panel and context menu go with it, and the commands that act on it are off (node search, edit, node and view keys); Run, file and window commands stay. Closing the Graph's area just closes it; Ctrl+Alt+G or Graph in any area's menu brings it back there. In the default one-area window, App in the Graph's menu runs the App | Graph switch (`view.toggle-app`) rather than making an App area. While the Graph is hidden the App | Graph switch has neither segment chosen (unless the App is shown) and Graph brings the Graph into the active area; Reset layout is on whenever the layout is not the default.
- Why: Blender's editor-type menu lets any area switch to any editor, with no editor pinned to a place; a window one person wants as Canvas alone should be possible, and the greyed row with a note taught a rule the person had to read. The Graph as the one exception was the odd thing out.
- Convention: Blender's editor-type menu (every area, every editor; Graph and App are one per window, as a Blender scene's editor of a single instance moves rather than copies).
- Rules met: Floor (the menu's rows are the WidgetMenu's, unchanged), Destructive (nothing is lost: the Graph and its state stay alive, hidden, and the area's state per view is kept), Words (no new strings; the note "the Graph keeps an area" is gone), Right-to-left (the menu is unchanged).

New: glyph `app-window`, a checkable toggle (`Accessible.checked` follows `listed`), lit glass when listed, no accent or ring (`KitFrost.on`, kit-wide: the shelf's pin `appBookmark-<id>` shows it too). Tips say "app launcher" (Omarchy's name, Super+Space); notices "Added to the app launcher" / "Taken out of the app launcher".

Press, Enter or Space puts `copyText` on the clipboard as plain text (`App.copyText`) and shows a check for `durCopied` (1.5 s; a swap, nothing animates, so reduced motion is unchanged); tooltip "Copy the message" / "Copied". Tab reaches it; the ring is for keys only.

An app, the shelf and the empty graph of Start blank keep "App". The segments share the widest label's width, so the switch is a little wider on a plain graph.

### 2026-11-25 App | Graph switch always shown; App's action per state (owner report; UI consult: confidence high, App opening the builder medium-high)

- Control: `src/apps/AppViewSwitch.qml` (`appViewSwitch`, `appViewSwitchApp`, `appViewSwitchGraph`), made by `src/menu/MenuBar.qml` (`appSwitchWanted` is only `appSwitchAllowed` and a bar at least 56u wide); `view.toggle-app` (Ctrl+Alt+A), whose handler is chosen by the tab's state (`bindAppView`, `bindViewSwitch` in `src/apps/app_window.cpp`); `AppShelf.showAgain()` and `AppShelf.landing`.
- Old: made only while the shown workflow had an app block ("hidden for a workflow with no app block"). Owner: "opening a fresh session of Fugue lacks the 'App / Graph' top button... I find it confusing."
- New: always shown, labels "App | Graph" in every state (only focus mode or a hidden bar removes it; a bar under 56u wide waits, as it would cover the menu button); pressing the chosen segment does nothing; Ctrl+Alt+A does exactly what the other segment does, and the Window and View menus' "App view" row is checked on the shelf. By state: the shelf (a pristine empty tab): App chosen, Graph is Start blank; an app workflow: as before (the App view and the graph); a plain graph with nodes: Graph chosen, App opens the app builder (`app.make`, tip "Make this workflow an app (Ctrl+Alt+M)"; cancelling leaves Graph chosen; "Apply and open App view" lands in the App view); the empty graph Start blank gave: Graph chosen, App brings the shelf back (`showAgain()`).
- The shelf's "Start blank" stays a session flag, not a tab's: `showAgain()` clears it, so every pristine tab shows the shelf again until the next Start blank. Smallest clean change (a per-tab flag needs a tab id in the shelf and a cleanup on close); the cost is that a second pristine tab follows the first's choice. Confidence medium-high.
- Convention named: Blender's always-present workspace tabs in the top bar (a mode switch the window always has, each tab a place to go); ComfyUI's switch only appears for an app workflow. Deviation from ComfyUI: the switch is there for every tab, and App on a plain graph is the way to make an app, so a person who has not met apps sees it and can find out. Keys: Tab reaches it, arrows, Space and Enter as KitSegmented; the focus ring is for keys only (FocusInput).
- Why: a control that appears and disappears with the document is a guess the person must make ("where did it go?"); a constant place with a constant label, whose other segment always goes somewhere useful, is learnable. Rules met: Floor, Primary (neutral), Learned place (unchanged: the bar's far end), Gaps (2u from the chip and the tabs).
- Not checked by a person: the switch on the shelf in a real window; the narrowest widths with many tabs; the right-to-left mirror.

### 2026-11-25 Providers page on the Drift kit; "Add a service…" with well-known services (owner; Opus review applied)

*Replaced the same day by the row above: a card per instance and the dropdown are gone; the keys, Remove question and field rules below still hold.*

- Control: `src/settings/ProvidersPage.qml` (per instance: a title line with the name and its Enabled `KitToggle`, one dim line (where it runs, or for a cloud service what it is sent), a `KitGroup` of `KitRow`s, and a `KitGroup` of its own for Remove; `providerAddService`, a `KitDropdown` opening a `WidgetMenu` of the presets by group; the key's eye inside its well; `providerKeyGet-N`; `providerAdvanced-N`; `providerRemoveAsk`), `SettingsParts.Field` (`adornment`, `reveal`, `label`), `KitChip.dimmed`, `WidgetMenu.maxRows`, `KitWell` (softer focus lift in the flat look), `SettingsPanel.reveal()` and its always-reserved scroll gutter; presets in `src/settings/provider_presets.{h,cpp}` (spec 8.2).
- Old: a hand-built card per instance (a row of name, type, small buttons, then a grid of labels and fields), one "Add <type> provider" button per type, a 34u key field with its buttons beside it, Remove in the card's last row.
- New: pick a service from "Add a service…" (a 64u menu showing every service and both headings at once; a cloud service already added says "Added" and choosing it goes to its card) and only the key is left: the card scrolls so its title sits 3u under the top, and the key field has the focus. A cloud card opens on Key (a 60u well, the eye inside it at the end, "Paste your fal.ai key"; the hint says "Needed before fal.ai can run" while it is on without one, "Saved" after), then "Get a key" (a link chip, the only network action; "Clear key" beside it when a key is stored), the service's own switch ("Let fal.ai keep my pictures"), Advanced (name, address, the module's rare settings), Order. Other cards keep Name, Address, Key. Every well ends on one right edge. The privacy line says "Runs in the cloud: your prompts and pictures go to fal.ai" (prompts only for OpenRouter). With no service left, one row says "No service yet" and holds the dropdown. A Move chip that cannot act dims and its tip says why. No "Make default" control existed on this page (the order is the preference), so Move up and down stay as round chips in an Order row.

A preset removes that step, and the privacy line puts the cost of choosing a cloud service where the choice is made (the consent question of 8.2 still asks before the first run). An Opus review of the first screenshots found the key buried under fields the person never needs, a key well showing 18 of 60 characters, wells ending on different edges, and Remove not a real destructive chunk.
- Rules met: Floor (chips 7u, the eye's press area 6u inside an 8u well: an exception, a 24px circle centred on it meets no other target), Primary (none on this page; the add dropdown is raised, not accent), Destructive (Remove in its own group, 5u after the rows' 1u gaps; with a stored key it asks a Question whose safe answer, Keep it, is highlighted and focused; with no key nothing is lost and it removes at once), Gaps (rows from the kit), Keys (Tab in reading order: switch, key, eye, Get a key, Clear key, the settings, Advanced, Order, Remove; the dropdown opens on Enter, Space or Down, Escape closes), Right-to-left (kit rows mirror), States (focus ring for the keyboard only; a field's edge lifts to 45% of `label`, 30% in the flat look), Words (sentence case; "Add a service…" has an ellipsis since a menu follows).

### 2026-11-25 App view: Compare lines the input up where it sits in the result (owner; decided by the coordinator, medium-high)

- Control: `src/apps/AppStage.qml` `appResultViewer` (`placeB`), `src/canvas/CompareView.qml` `placeB`, `AppView::inputPlacement`; the app block's `compare` (spec 4.9).
- Old: Compare fitted the input to the result's frame, so a 1024 x 1024 input stretched over a taller expanded result.
- New: an app that grows a picture names the picture and the padding inputs in `compare`; Compare shows the result whole and the input at its own place in it (a square stays square, the added area shows around it), for slider and flip; side by side and difference keep the old fit.
- 100% while comparing is of the larger picture (owner, same day): by area as before; with a placement, by density (the input's file pixels per result pixel over its place), so an input as dense as the result or denser is at 100% of its own pixels, a sparser one leaves A's. Z, 1 and the 100% chip already take the larger first; a second press toggles A and B as in the Compare node.
- Why: the owner's ask (the square fully visible, the extension above and below, as a sliding A/B); the Compare Images node's fit is right for two pictures of one subject, wrong for a picture and its extension. Placement comes from the take's values, so it stays right after the form changes. Not asked of the graph's Compare node: it has no padding to read.

### 2026-11-25 Scroll bar: a calm pill in a 2.5u gutter, kept out of the content (owner's feedback)

- Control: `src/canvas/FugueScrollBar.qml`; hosts `src/apps/AppPage.qml` (`appForm`), `AppLibrary.qml`, `AppBuilderDialog.qml` (`builderPreview`), `AppTakes.qml`.

- New: a 2.5u gutter, all of it the hit area; no track at rest, `kitTrack` while hovered or pressed; a round `label` pill at least 8u long, 1u wide (1.5u hovered or pressed), alpha 0.35 (0.45 light) at rest, 0.7 hovered, 0.9 pressed (`thumbAlpha`, `thumbAlphaLight`, `thumbHover`, `thumbPress`), animated over `durColor`. A host padded by at least 2.5u on that side holds the bar in the padding (the App form does not shrink); others pad by `inset`. Mirrors in right-to-left; takes no focus.

### 2026-11-25 Focus rings are for the keyboard; a text field never draws one (owner)

- Control: new `src/focus_input.h` (`FocusInput.keyboard`, an application event filter: a key press sets it, a mouse, touch or tablet press clears it); `src/kit/KitWell.qml` (the `focused` edge), `src/canvas/ThemedTextField.qml` (own background, no Material accent underline), `CanvasTaskBar` prompt, `CommandUi` filter, `DragNumberField` while typing; the ring (`activeFocus && FocusInput.keyboard`) in `KitSegmented`, `KitSlider`, `KitDropdown`, `KitChip`, `KitToggle`, `KitHero`, `ViewButton`, `ThemedDropDown`, `ValueSlider`, `MenuBar`, `AreaHeader`, `SettingsPanel`, `GetReadyRow`, `TemplatesDialog`, `AppRail`, `AppImageInput`, layer and history lists, `MediaView`, `MediaThumb`, `ColorPickerPane`, `RowTrashButton`, `SidePanel`, `SideTabStrip`, `RunToolbar`.
- Old: the Apps dialog's search field (it takes the focus on opening) drew a 2px accent ring, a pale cream in the owner's theme, read as a white surround; every control drew the ring after a click.
- New: a text field never draws the accent ring; focused, its edge is 1px in `label` at 45% (no width change, so nothing jumps) and the caret shows where typing goes. Every other control draws the 2px accent ring only when the last input was the keyboard (Tab, arrows), as a browser's :focus-visible; a click, or focus given on opening by a click, shows none, and the next key press shows it on the focused control.
- Why: Item has no focusReason in QML (only Control has), and a per-control "pressFocus" mark (the earlier App | Graph fix) cannot see focus given on opening; one application-wide "last input" flag does, in one place. Confidence medium-high (coordinator's call): a dialog opened by a shortcut still shows its default control's ring, which keeps "every dialog works by keyboard alone, with a visible ring".

### 2026-11-25 App view: Clear empties the takes; Repeat needs a seed that varies; one raised dropdown; a slider's value is typed (owner's second look)

- Control: `src/apps/AppStage.qml` `appClear` (now also when only the strip has takes); `src/apps/AppPage.qml` `appLiveMode` (Repeat only when `AppView.canRepeat`), `appRepeatWaits`; `src/kit/KitDropdown.qml` (new) for `appImageChoose-<key>`, `AppChoice.qml` and `AppModelInput.qml` (`appInput-<key>`); `src/kit/KitSlider.qml` the value field `<objectName>-value` (App view ranges, Settings > Look sliders).

- New: Clear empties both, records the newest run cleared, the run folders stay (History, Results); Repeat is not offered without a seed, and with every seed Fixed it runs once and waits with one quiet line; every dropdown is a `KitDropdown`: raised (it is clicked), text and chevron each 2.5u from the ends (canvas-look 20, Chip padding); a slider's value is a 6u `KitWell` field at the rail's end: Enter or leaving it takes the number (in range, on the steps), Escape restores, Up and Down step.

### 2026-11-25 Frosted chips over a hero: real glass, not a dark disc (owner's screenshot)

- Control: `src/kit/KitFrost.qml` (the glass of every `KitChip` with `frosted`, the hero's status pill and close button), seen on the App view's `appLauncherToggle`, `appKeep`, `appHideInputs`, `appOpenInFugue`.
- Old: the chips came out near-black opaque discs. `measure()` asked `backdrop.window`, which an Item does not have in QML, so the picture under the glass was never measured and the glass kept its unmeasured plan; that plan's dark base saturated at 80% black because the contrast estimate mixed the white tint in linear light (it can never get white ink under 4.5:1 that way).
- New: the window is asked through `Window.window`, so the measuring runs; the base and tint are mixed on the gamma-encoded colour, as the scene graph blends them, so over a dark hero the base stays a light smoke and the translucent tint and blur show. Frost at 0 is unchanged: the plain opaque chip. Size and press targets unchanged (7u, above the 6u floor); keys, focus ring and tooltips unchanged.

### 2026-11-25 App view: the inputs panel resizes and slides shut; the toolbar at the card's edge; the focus ring for keys only; the project chip (owner's look at the night build)

- Old: the panel was a quarter of the window, fixed; the toolbar sat 2px + 1u lower than the card's side margin (the progress row and its spacing, empty while idle); a click on App | Graph left an accent outline on it; the project chip was a one-off 6u square-cornered outline with accent text in a project, and read "Default project" in the Default once any project existed.
- New: drag the border (or Tab to it and use Left/Right, Shift 5x) to resize, 80u to what leaves the stage 100u; Enter or a double click gives the automatic width back; a drag under half the least width hides the panel. Hidden, the card slides to the strip (look `durThumb`, 0 under reduced motion), the form clipped and moving with the card's edge; Show the inputs slides it back. The keyboard's place follows: hide by keys and the focus goes to Show the inputs, and back.
