Run stays on the strip (Same surface, Fitts) because a hidden form must not hide the primary. Keyboard focus rings follow the web's `:focus-visible`: a ring after a click says nothing the click did not.
- Rules met: Floor (the handle 24px across, the chips 7u), Primary (one accent: the form's Run while shown, the strip's Run while shut, never both), Gaps (the cards' 2u gap is the handle's place; the strip's 1u), Learned place (Window > App inputs beside App view), Keys (no new key: the registry's N is the node library and the App view's bare keys are the page's; the border by Tab and arrows, the panel by Tab to its buttons, the menu), Destructive (none: hiding loses nothing, the values stay).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): no key for App inputs (Blender's N is taken by the node library; medium); the strip keeps Run (high); a drag under half the least width hides (Blender's feel; medium); the width is one for all apps, not per app (medium); the project chip stays at the far end of the bar, not moved next to the menus as the UI review suggested (scope order File… project… tabs: medium; left for the owner); no glyph on the chip (the subset's `folder`, `box` and `layers` mean other things; a `briefcase` glyph needs a font recipe).

### 2026-11-25 Make an app: the builder (#393, epic #397)

- Control: `src/apps/AppBuilderDialog.qml` (`appBuilderDialog`; `builderTitle`, `builderDescription`, `builderView`, `builderPick`, `builderAddInput`, `builderAddOutput`, `builderLabel-<n>`, `builderAdvanced-<n>`, `builderUp-<n>`, `builderDown-<n>`, `builderRemove-<n>`, `builderMin-<n>`, `builderMax-<n>`, `builderAlso-<n>`, `builderOutputLabel-<n>`, `builderPreview`, `builderApply`, `builderApplyOpen`, `builderRemoveApp`, `builderCancel`) on a `Question` with the sea hero, and `src/canvas/AppPickLayer.qml` (`appPickLayer`, `appPickArea`) with its banner (`appPickBanner`, `appPickDone`); the commands `app.make` (Ctrl+Alt+M, File > Make app…) and `app.edit` (File > Edit app…, shown only with a block). Store: `apps/app_builder.h`; spec 12.19.
- Old: an app block was written by hand in JSON, or came with a template.
- New: one dialog with two panes (what the app is, its inputs and its outputs on the start side; the live App-view form on the other), inputs and outputs added by clicking them on the canvas (the dialog steps aside, a banner with Done says how to leave) or from two lists, and one undo step on Apply.
- Convention named: ComfyUI's app builder (behaviour only: pick inputs, pick outputs, preview, choose the default view; its words are not used) and Fugue's own group interface editor (5.4: label, section, range, order, one input driving several), whose rows this dialog repeats; Blender's modal pick (the pointer outlines what a click takes, Escape leaves) for the canvas part.
- Why not the group editor itself: it edits a group's face, reads `groupInterface()` and needs a group; an app's inputs may sit on any node, carry roles and a default view and are checked by `checkApp`. So the dialog is new, and the rules and words are the editor's. The reuse is in `app::` (block, check, prune) and in the shape of the input rows.
- Rules met: Floor (every control 7u or more: the kit field, chips, toggle and the chevron buttons), Primary (Apply and open App view the one accent; Apply and Cancel plain), Destructive (Remove app only when editing, flat in the danger tint, one undo step takes it back, no confirm), D9 (the primary last at the right, Cancel before it), Gaps (the kit's own row spacing), Keys (Ctrl+Alt+M checked against the registry: unused; Tab order is reading order; Enter in a field applies, Escape cancels; Alt+Up and Alt+Down move a row), Learned place (File menu after All apps…), Words (sentence case: "Make an app" in the header, "Make app…" in the menu), Right-to-left (the dialog mirrors as a Question does).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): one command and one key for Make and for Edit (Edit is a menu row for the same dialog, shown only with a block), confidence high; plain Apply leaves the window where it was and Apply and open shows the App view, high; the list of inputs is sorted by node title, not canvas position, medium; choices, `optional`, `fallback` and section names other than Advanced are left to the JSON (the app form folds only Advanced), medium; the preview is a second idle `AppView`, so it is the real form and cannot drift from it, medium.

### 2026-11-24 App | Graph switch in the window bar (epic #397)

- Control: `src/apps/AppViewSwitch.qml` (`appViewSwitch`; `appViewSwitchApp`, `appViewSwitchGraph`) made by `src/menu/MenuBar.qml` only while the shown workflow has an app block; the command `view.toggle-app` (Ctrl+Alt+A) with rows in the Window menu (first) and the Graph's View menu (first), as "App view" with a check while the App view is shown.
- Old: the way back from Open in Graph was a strip under the canvas, "Show as app", only in the graph. Nothing in the App view said the graph was one click away but Open in Graph, and in the graph the strip was easy to miss (and gone in focus mode).
- New: the strip is removed. In the window bar's far end, 2u before the project chip, two segments **App | Graph** (6u tall, the view in force on the body fill and semibold, the other plain; a 1px hairline frame), in both views, so the person flips back and forth without moving to find a different control. Pressing the segment in force does nothing. Hidden for a workflow with no app block. With several areas (#450) it is shown too and reads the active area (App in force while that area shows the App); choosing the other trades that area's view (`Workspace::toggleApp`). Open in Graph in the App page's header stays (the page's own, first-time way) and runs the same switch.
- Convention named: the App | Graph switcher of ComfyUI's frontend (behaviour only: a segmented switch of the workflow's two views, in the top chrome, shown only for a workflow with an app); Blender's workspace tabs in the top bar for the same reason (a mode switch belongs to the window, not to a view). Deviations: a command and a key as well (Ctrl+Alt+A, free: Ctrl+Alt+G/C/N/W/Enter are taken by their own rows), and the check in the menus.
- Why: the switch is a mode of the window's whole body, so it lives in the window bar, which both views share (the Graph's area header does not exist in the App view); a segmented control shows both states at once and the one in force, a button would have to change its label. The graph is the loose end of the same row as the tabs, so it is also where the eye already looks for "which workflow".
- Rules met: Floor (6u/24px tall, each segment its label and 2.5u each side, over the 24px minimum on the height), Primary (neutral: Run is the App view's one primary, the accent is only the keyboard focus ring), Destructive (none), Gaps (2u before the chip and after the tabs, as the chip's own gap), Learned place (the far end of the window bar, next to the project chip, the other per-window state), Keys (Ctrl+Alt+A checked against the registry: unused; Tab reaches both segments, Space or Enter picks), Posture (the pointer is already up in the bar for tabs and menus).
- Cause of the report ("empty node canvas" after Open in Graph): a canvas the shelf or the App view hides is not laid out, so a workflow opened meanwhile was placed (view centre and fit) for the canvas's old size; with the window resized or tiled since, the nodes landed off to the side or out of sight. The canvas now places the document again at its new size when it is shown (`FugueCanvas.placeAgain`).
- Open choices (owner, to confirm): the key; whether the switch should also be in the Graph's area header when the window bar is hidden (it is not: View > App view and the key reach it); Open in Graph on the App page stays as the page's own button.
- Not checked by a person: the switch in a real window next to the project chip and with many tabs at the narrowest width; the right-to-left mirror.

### 2026-11-24 Grouped rows as the convention for settings-like surfaces: the Drift kit (epic #456, slice 1)

- Control: `src/kit/Kit*.qml` (`KitSection`, `KitGroup`, `KitRow`, `KitSeparator`, `KitToggle`, `KitChip`, `KitSlider`, `KitHero`); the section "Drift kit" of canvas-look.md is the spec. Nothing in the window uses them yet; slices 2 to 5 move the App view, the shelf, Settings and the sheets onto them.
- Old: each surface made its own rows (`SettingsParts`, per-panel layouts).
- New: a surface that is a list of settings or inputs is groups of rows, as iOS settings and omarchy-drift are: a bold small section title, a rounded group, rows with a label and a dim one-line hint at the start, the control at the end, a hairline between rows, the whole row a hit area for a switch.
- Convention named: iOS Settings (grouped table cells, a switch at the end of a row that also takes the row's tap) and omarchy-drift (the owner's own app, the look reference). Deviation: the controls are controlled (the model owns the value), the colors follow the Omarchy theme and not fixed greys.
- Rules met: Floor (switch 10u by 7u to press, chip 7u, slider 7u, rows 10u or more; the hero's pill and close button are 6u = 24px), Primary (the kit fills nothing with the accent but a switch that is on and a slider's filled rail; a surface's one primary stays a `ViewButton`), Destructive (none in the kit), Gaps (rows 3u inside, 1.5u between a section title and its group, 5u before a title), Learned place (nothing moves), Posture (these are transient and form surfaces: few decisions per row). Keys: Tab reaches each control; Space flips a switch and presses a chip; arrows, PageUp/Down, Home and End move a slider.

### 2026-11-24 Apps shelf and Templates window in the Drift kit; search, pins, copies, names; moving previews (#463, #470, epic #456 slice 3)

- Control: `src/apps/AppShelfView.qml` (`appShelfView`, `appsHero`, `appsSearch`, `appsBrowseTemplates`, `appsStartBlank`, `appsDialogBrowse`) holds the page and the dialog (`AppsHome.qml`, `AppsDialog.qml`), over `AppShelfGrid.qml` (`appShelfGrid`, `appCardMenu`) of `AppCard.qml` (`appCard-<id>`, `appPicture-<id>`, `appBookmark-<id>`, `appMore-<id>`, `appRename-<id>`); the Templates window `src/workflow/TemplatesDialog.qml` (`templatesPanel`, `templatesHero`, `templatesSearch`, `templatesReadyOnly`, `templatesApps`, `templateCard-<id>`, `templateInfo-<id>`; `FilterChip.qml` is gone, the chips are `KitChip`s). New kit parts: `KitCard`, `KitPicture`, `KitVideo`, `KitSearch`, `KitChip.toned`, `KitHero.video`.
- Old: a popup surface with its own search box, chips and flat cards; cards without a search, a pin, a copy or a name of their own.
- New: Apps is a Dusk panel, Templates a Sea panel, each with a `KitHero` header (serif title, the buttons frosted over it); a search field is a recessed well with a clear button; a card is raised (light top edge, soft shadow), corners and gaps follow the Corners and Spacing sliders, the Flat preset gives square, plain cards from the same code; filter chips are raised, and take the window's tone as translucent colour while on; a card's picture moves while in view (the app's `preview.webm`), the buttons over it (pin, menu, info) are frosted with adaptive ink; the search narrows the shelf as you type; a pinned app comes first; Duplicate and Rename live on the card's menu and keys.
- Rules met: Floor (chips and the search 7u, the round buttons 7u, a card far above), Primary (none on the shelf: the accent stays Run's), Gaps (cards `areaGap` apart, padding `rowPadY`, the search 3u under the header), Learned place (Browse templates and Start blank keep their place at the header's end), Posture (browsing: a calm page; the dialog transient). Keys: the search has the focus on open; Tab and Shift+Tab walk the header's buttons, the search and the cards in a ring (set by hand, because the window's own Tab is "Add a node by search" and its focus chain skips the shelf at home); the arrows move over the cards, Up from the first row returns to the search; Enter or Space open, F2 renames an own app, Ctrl+D duplicates, Ctrl+B pins, Menu or Shift+F10 the card's menu; Escape closes the dialog (it does not clear the search: the field's clear button does); in the Templates window the search, the categories and the cards keep the keys they had. `test_app_shelf_window` drives them.
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): a moving preview plays only while its card is hovered or has the keyboard's place, the window focused and motion normal (decided by the coordinator overnight for the owner's "calm" and "not taxing" rules: one clip at a time); the Templates window's cards move only for the one under the pointer or the keyboard (a big catalog); the pin uses the `pin` glyph (the Lucide subset has no `bookmark`; a font recipe adds one); the shelf's Duplicate names the copy "<name> copy" and opens it at once, so a person edits it as an App view; Rename edits files of the person's own template in place, even one open in a tab (the tab keeps its title until reopened).
- Not checked by a person: the clips playing in a real window (the stills are the grabs), the hover look, the light themes on the real GPU.

### 2026-11-24 Apps shelf: the door for the hobbyist (#388, epic #397)

- Control: `src/apps/AppsHome.qml` (`appsHome`; `appsStartBlank`, `appsBrowseTemplates`), the dialog `src/apps/AppsDialog.qml` (`appsDialog`; `appsDialogBrowse`) for File > Apps… (`app.show-all`, after Browse templates, before Projects…), both over `src/apps/AppShelfGrid.qml` (`appShelfGrid`, `appShelfEmpty`) of `src/apps/AppCard.qml` (`appCard-<id>`, `appTitle-<id>`, `appSummary-<id>`, `appReady-<id>`, `appPlaces-<id>`). Store: `apps/app_shelf.h`.
- Old: Fugue has no start screen; a window with nothing open is an empty graph, and apps were found only through File > Browse templates and its Apps chip.
- New: a window with nothing open (one empty, untitled workflow) shows the shelf in the canvas's place, as the App view does: a title and one line, **Browse templates** and **Start blank** at the end of the top row, and the apps as cards in a grid (56u or wider, the preview at 16:10, the name, up to three lines of what it does, then one line: a check and "Ready", or what is missing, "Needs 2.1 GB and 1 pack"; the Templates window's where-badges on the picture). Ready apps first. The whole card is one button: it opens the app in the App view; an app that is not ready opens the same tab and the one Get ready sheet (#379) over it, and on its yes the person is in the App view, ready to Run. **Start blank** gives the empty graph for the rest of the session. File > Apps… is the same grid in a dialog over open tabs. A window given a file, or that reopens its tabs, never makes the shelf: it reads nothing and builds nothing.
- Convention named: the empty-state start page of Photoshop's Home screen (quick starts and recent work in the window's body while no document is open, gone once one is) with Blender's way out (a splash over the default scene, one click to carry on blank). Researched from their docs only. Deviations, each with its why: a page in the body, not a modal splash (a modal over a first window costs a click every launch, and the canvas stays unmoved behind it); no Recent files list yet (Workflows' tab restore already brings last work back, so the shelf is only for starting new); no "show at start" setting yet (see the open choices).
- Why: the hobbyist's first question is "what can I make", and an app is the answer, so the shelf is the first thing seen and shows only apps; the pro, who opens files directly, never sees it (command line, restored tabs) and gets out of it with one click or by opening anything. One catalog: the cards read `Workflows::templates()` filtered to `app`, so a download, a host answering or a new template changes both windows at once. The readiness line says what the one sheet will ask for before the person chooses, so no sheet comes as a surprise, and a not-ready app is still one click: the sheet is the one yes, not four (#379).
- Rules met: Floor (a card at least 56u wide and as tall as its picture, name and lines need, Start blank and Browse templates 7u ViewButtons, the Browse templates button 7u in the dialog), Primary (no accent fill: choosing an app is the page's job, a card is not a command; Run stays the App view's one primary), Destructive (none; nothing is installed before the sheet's one yes), Gaps (card margin 1u, a card's inner gap 1u, the top row's 3u between its chunks), Learned place (File items before it keep their order; Browse templates keeps its place), Posture (the shelf is a page, transient by nature: fewer decisions, the large target is the whole card; the dialog is transient too). Keys: Tab reaches the grid, arrows move, Enter or Space open the focused card; Escape closes the dialog.
- Open choices (owner, to confirm): a Settings > General "Show apps when nothing is open" toggle (Photoshop has one; this ships without, the cost to a pro who starts empty being one click on Start blank); whether the shelf also shows Recent files; the card's "i" details sheet is left to the Templates window.
- Not checked by a person: the shelf in a real window, its look at the narrowest width, and the cards with real previews.

### 2026-11-25 Node cards: Compact (Drift) and Flat, the Node style setting (#466, epic #456 slice 6)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasNode.qml` (the face), `src/canvas/widgets/InputWidget.qml`, `ToggleSwitch.qml`, `SliderFill.qml`, `src/canvas/Thumbnail.qml`; Settings > Theme > Look, `nodeStyle` (a `KitSegmented`: Compact, Flat), setting `node_style`; the fold chevron `foldChevron` on a Compact header.
- Old: one node face, the Blender-like one (canvas-look.md sections 4 to 9).
- New: **Node style: Compact / Flat**, live, per user (settings.json `node_style`, default `compact`). Compact is the Drift card: 12px corners, a hairline border, a soft shadow, a 28px header with a kind dot, the name at 13px semibold and a fold chevron, 26px rows with the label dim at 12px on the left and the control on the right (a recessed value well 20px high, a 32 x 18px pill for a toggle, a tone-filled well for a slider), 10px port dots on the card's edge, a faint Dusk tint, a thin progress line and the percentage while running, an accent ring when selected, pictures with a size badge. Flat is today's face, unchanged. The Flat look preset also sets Node style to Flat (the preset file carries it as data, `"settings": {"node_style": "flat"}`); the two can be mixed.
- Why: the owner asked for the same feel, more compact and still easily readable, and for the Blender-like face to stay for the people who arrive knowing it (Two audiences). Only the face's metrics and drawing switch; the model, the ports, the wires, the sizes in the file, the keys and the gestures are shared, so switching reloads nothing and changes no document.
- Rules met: Floor (a Compact row is 26px, its well and pill 20 and 18px inside it, the whole row is the press target as before: the widget's mouse area spans the control; the chevron's area is 14px, an exception, the same fold is on the node menu and the Collapse key), Primary (none on a node), Destructive (none), Contrast (labels `label` on the card: 4.5:1 as the Flat face), Keys (Tab and Enter in a Compact widget are the Flat widget's: tested).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): Compact is 56u wide (224px) by default, 6.5u rows and half a unit above the first row (the board's 204 to 244px; ~15% narrower than its first pass was the owner's note), the sizes in a file win in both styles (confidence high); the board puts an input and an output on one row, the model keeps one row per port (the ports, wires and hit tests share one layout, so a row-sharing layout is a model change for its own ticket; medium); `KitToggle`, `KitSlider` and `KitWell` are not instanced on a node's face: they take their own presses and carry halos and extra items, and the canvas takes the presses of a node and counts its items (12.1), so the face draws the same recipes from the same look parameters in a few items (high; the pill, the well's fill, edge, top shadow and bottom light, the tone fill); the kind dot is the kind mark (the first output's port colour and shape, so colour is not the only signal), not a category colour, as the model has no category colour (medium); the running pulse of the edge is kept in both styles; the labels' column is a third of the card at least and half at most, wider for the longest label of a row with a control (medium).

### 2026-11-24 App view restyled with the Drift kit (epic #456 slice 2; owner feedback on builds 1 and 2)
- Control: `src/apps/AppPage.qml` (three rounded cards 2u apart: `appRail`, the stage, `appPanel`), `AppRail.qml` (icon over a word: Apps first, Results, Graph; tooltip "All apps (Ctrl+Shift+A)"), the panel's `KitHero` (`appTitle`: preview picture, serif title, one-line description; `appLauncherToggle` and `appOpenInFugue` as frosted round chips) over `KitSection`/`KitGroup` rows (`AppInput`: `KitRow` for a switch and a choice, `AppRow` for a picture, prompt, number, slider, seed, mask and model; `appAdvanced` a disclosure row) and `appRun`, a full-width accent pill; the stage toolbar and the takes strip in `KitChip`s; Fugue's mark (`fugueMark`) at the start of the window bar.
- New: the window's side tab strip and panels are not shown in the App view (the rail is the only rail); a click on the picture well only focuses it (Browse opens the picker); `app.show-all` (Ctrl+Shift+A, File > All apps…) opens the shelf on the current app's card from any view; the rail's Graph item runs `view.toggle-app`.
- Keys: Tab walks the panel in reading order (hero buttons, inputs, Advanced, Run) with a visible ring; bare keys (Tab, letters) belong to the page while the App view is shown, so Tab no longer opens node search; Enter or Space presses the focused button, Ctrl+Enter runs from anywhere, Down opens the picture or choice menu and Escape closes it, Left and Right move in the takes strip (`test_app_window` drives them).
- Rules met: Floor (chips and the rail items 7u or more, Run 9u), Primary (Run, the one accent pill), Gaps (2u between cards, 3u inside the panel), Learned place (Apps first on the rail, the mark where the app's name sits in other windows), Posture (a form on a quiet card).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): the Apps rail glyph stays `layout-template` (the bundled Lucide subset has no grid glyph; adding one is a font recipe); no per-input hint exists in the app block, so `KitRow`'s hint shows only when a field gives `hint`; the slider has no reset-on-double-click (KitSlider has none); choice and picture dropdowns are raised chips, not wells, because they are clicked; the slider shows its value in the row's heading, not in a box.

### 2026-11-24 The Drift look rules: one calm look for every surface (epic #456, brand Dusk)

- Control: the spec is docs/canvas-look.md section 20 (recipes, tokens, status per element); the kit is `src/kit/`.
- Old: each surface drew its own rows, buttons and toggles; flat greys.
- New: clickable is raised, fillable is recessed; over a picture frosted (adaptive ink, 4.5:1) and smoked glass, Frost 0 makes it opaque; headers fade softly into tinted panels (Panel colour: Tinted or Plain), header tones by area; toggles, choosers and sliders are glass in the panel's tone with a faint halo; every space is a rounded card with a 2u gap; node cards Compact or Flat (setting). Rejected: layered-glass header panes, Voices and Ink brands.
- Keys: every dialog, sheet and popover: Tab order with a visible ring, first useful control focused, Enter default, Escape closes and returns focus, arrows in lists and choosers, Space flips and presses; one key-only window test per dialog.
- Rules met: Floor (7u targets, 6u hero buttons), Primary (one accent pill per screen), Gaps (2u between spaces), Posture (calm surfaces).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): the slate and ember glass colours and every light-theme derivation are proposals checked in the first grabs; Run stays the accent pill, not the board's tone-gradient button (confidence high: one primary rule); node value controls are wells, not the Nodes board's flat chips (high: the owner's fillable rule); the smoked fill follows the board's numbers, not the code's (medium).

### 2026-11-24 App view: a workflow as one simple screen (#385, epic #397)

- Control: `src/apps/AppPage.qml` (`appPage`; `appOpenInGraph`, `appForm`, `appAdvanced`, `appRun`, `appCancel`, `appProgressText`, `appError`, `appDetails`, `appResults`), `src/apps/AppInput.qml` (`appInput-<key>`), `src/apps/AppResultDialog.qml` (`appResultDialog`), and, since #397, the window bar's App | Graph switch (below) in place of the strip `appBackHost` with **Show as app**.
- Old: every workflow opens as the graph.
- New: a workflow with an `app` block whose `defaultView` is `app` opens as a form and its output, in the canvas's place (a tab mode, one area). Title and **Open in Graph** on the top row; the form at the start, 52u wide, at most; the output filling the rest. Under the form, **Run** (the accent fill, the form's full width, 7u) and, only while a run waits or goes, **Cancel** (danger) in the same row, 3u from Run; one line of progress and a 2px bar under them. The output holds the results, or "The result appears here.". A failed run shows one line in the error color at the top of the output and a quiet **Details** (a flat toggle) for the rest. The window with no app block has none of these items (`tests/workspace/data/default-window.txt`, unchanged).
- Why: the screen is the product, a task and nothing else (Two audiences: no graph or node word on it, Open in Graph the one way out, named as the epic names it). Run is the one primary control and sits last under the form, where the eye ends after the inputs (Fitts: full width, a large target; the key stays Ctrl+Enter, spec 12.3). Cancel is destructive: its own chunk, 3u from Run against 0 inside a chunk, and hidden until it can act (Hick). Details and Advanced are folded: most runs never need them. Blender's and Photoshop's conventions do not decide here (no counterpart); the reference is ComfyUI's App Mode (inputs panel, results beside it, Graph one click away), read from its public docs only, and Fugue differs by keeping Cancel and the progress line in the form's column so the output keeps its room.
- Rules met: Floor (Run, Cancel, Open in Graph, Details, Advanced and Show as app 24px or more: ViewButton 7u/28px, 6u/24px compact for Show as app; a text field and a drop-down 28px; the drag number 28px), Primary (Run, one on the screen; Open in Graph is bordered, not filled), Destructive (Cancel in its own chunk, 3u from Run, shown only while it can act; cancelling loses no data, the form keeps its values), Gaps (1u between a label and its control, 3u between inputs and between chunks), Learned place (Ctrl+Enter, Ctrl+Alt+Enter and Escape of the Run menu keep their keys and act on the app while it is shown), Posture (the form is a surface for a person who is not editing a graph: few decisions, one primary).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): clickable things are raised and fillable things are recessed (owner rule, 2026-11-24: chips, buttons, rail items and Run get a light top edge and a soft shadow, fields and wells get the `kit-well` fill with an inner top shadow, never the other way round); the App view is a mode of the tab and not a view type of an area until #217 carries it (the epic's decision 2); Show as app is a strip under the canvas, only for an app tab, because a menu item would change the menus' budget and the default window's items; a block that does not read opens as the graph with no word (checking it belongs to #387/#393); a number with no range is a text field and a number with `min` and `max` the drag field the Canvas view uses; the first of a choice's words is in force when the block names no default; a file input shows its path as text until #386 (done below).

### 2026-11-24 App view: one control per input type (#386, epic #397)

- Control: `src/apps/AppInput.qml` chooses; `AppTextArea.qml` (`appInput-<key>`, editor `appInput-<key>-edit`), `AppToggle.qml`, `AppSeedInput.qml` (`appInput-<key>`, `appSeedRoll-<key>`, `appSeedMode-<key>`), `AppImageInput.qml` (`appInput-<key>` the well, `appImageBrowse-<key>`, `appImagePaste-<key>`, `appImageStrip-<key>`, `appImageNotice-<key>`), `AppMaskInput.qml` (`appInput-<key>` the button, `appMaskState-<key>`, `appMaskClear-<key>`), `AppModelInput.qml` (`appInput-<key>`), and `canvas/widgets/ModelDownloadAsk.qml` (`downloadAsk`, taken out of `WidgetPicker.qml` so the node's picker and the form ask the same question).
- Old: a text field for every kind except a number with a range and a choice; a picture, a mask, a seed and a model were typed as text, a switch was Yes or No.
- New: a prompt is a well of 3 to 8 lines; a toggle is a 4.5u x 8u switch with "On" or "Off" beside it; a seed is the number, a dice (`ViewButton`, 28px) and a drop-down of its three modes in one row, 1u apart; a picture is a 36u well that shows it and takes a drop, with **Browse...** and **Paste** under it and a strip of 14u thumbnails (the template's samples, then the newest in the inputs folder; the chosen one has the accent edge); a mask is **Paint mask...** with "Painted" or "None" and a flat **Clear**; a model is a 28px well with the name and a chevron that opens the node picker's menu.
- Why: each kind is the control the person already knows from the node faces, so nothing new is learned, but none shows a node or port word. A drop target is the largest on the form (Fitts), pasting and browsing are the two other ways in, and the strip answers "last used" and the template's samples in one place (Hick: ten at most). A seed keeps the node's three modes because the choice is the same one; Fixed, Randomize and Increment read as in the graph. Download asks first because it fetches gigabytes and a license (as in the graph).
- Rules met: Floor (the well 36u, buttons, fields, drop-downs and the model well 28px or more, thumbnails 56px), Primary (none added: Run stays the form's one), Destructive (Clear takes a mask away, one tap, undone by painting again; nothing else removes data), Gaps (1u inside a control, 3u between inputs as before), Learned place (the node picker's menu and keys, the mask editor of 14.4, the Load Image paste and browse), Posture (a form: few, large targets). Keys: Tab reaches every control in order, Enter or Space opens or flips it, Left and Right move along the strip, Ctrl+V pastes on the picture well; Tab leaves a prompt instead of typing a tab, and Ctrl+Enter reaches the window's Run from a prompt and from a one-line field (`test_app_window`, it had been unverified).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): a seed starts in the mode the workflow has (a seed left to the engine is Randomize, a number is Fixed) and a number typed makes it Fixed; Increment wraps at `max`; the mask is painted over the form's first picture input; changing the picture keeps the mask; the strip shows the inputs folder's newest pictures for any picture input; the model list has no "fits this model" judging and no family filter (the node picker's), only the Recommended mark.

### 2026-11-24 An app as its own application (#398, epic #397)

- Control: `src/apps/AppWindow.qml` (`appWindow`; `appNotAnApp`, `appNotAnAppOpen`, the notice strip `appNotice` with `appGetReady` and `appNoticeDismiss`), and in `AppPage.qml` the quiet header buttons `appLauncherToggle` (Add to launcher, Remove from launcher) and `appOpenInFugue` (the app's own window only). The window opens with `fugue --app <id|file|folder>`.
- Old: an app was a tab of the full window (menu bar, tabs, panels around it) with Open in Graph its one way out.
- New: a window that is the App page and nothing else: no menu bar, tabs, panels, graph or node library, titled with the app's own title and with its preview as the window icon. In the page's header, next to the title's far end, two flat 7u-tall buttons: Add to launcher (`app-window`; Remove from launcher while the entry exists) and, in the app's own window only, Open in Fugue (the full window with the workflow as a graph, in a process of its own). In the full window's App view the header has Add to launcher beside the bordered Open in Graph. One notice line (8u) at the foot, only while the workflow lacks something or a launcher entry was made or removed, with **Get ready…** (primary) opening the #379 sheet. A file with no usable app block shows "This is not an app." with a primary Open in Fugue.
- Why: the owner asked for "an Application without all the Fugue editing stuff", for the hobbyist end of the two audiences: one task, one window, nothing of the graph. Both extra actions are about the app, not the task, so they are flat and at the far end of the header, away from Run and the form, where the eye does not go (Hick: the screen's decisions stay the form and Run). Closing the window asks nothing: the app's tab is a template or a file the window never edits, and a close that asked about "unsaved work" would show the graph's vocabulary. The reference is a desktop application: its own icon and title in the launcher and the task bar, with the Wayland app id equal to the entry's name (`fugue-app-<key>`) so the compositor ties the window to its entry.
- Rules met: Floor (the header buttons, Get ready… and Dismiss are ViewButton 7u/28px), Primary (Run is still the form's one; Get ready… is the primary only on the notice strip, which is not on the form; "This is not an app." has its own one), Destructive (Remove from launcher takes a file the app made for the launcher, one tap, undone by Add to launcher; nothing else is removed), Gaps (2u between the header buttons, 3u as before elsewhere), Learned place (Ctrl+Enter, Ctrl+Alt+Enter and `?` work as in the full window; Ctrl+Q and the window's close quit at once), Posture (a window for a person who is not editing).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): one process per app window, since Fugue has no single-instance plumbing (the helper and the model cache are shared across processes already, so a second app window starts warm); Open in Fugue starts `fugue --edit <ref>` and not `fugue <file>`, so a template (a folder in a read-only install) is opened as a new unnamed workflow and never the bundled file; the launcher entry's Exec is `fugue --app <ref>` (the AppImage's own path when run from one); a template of the catalog is named by its id and anything else by its absolute path; the icon is a copy of the app's `preview.*` in `$XDG_DATA_HOME/fugue/app-icons`, `fugue` when the app has none; the window keeps no session and no recovery copies (a scratch folder), and does not record itself as the last project.

### 2026-11-24 App view: paste a picture, Clear, a compact seed (owner's first look at #385 to #398)

- Old: Ctrl+V did nothing in the App view (the graph's paste ran on a hidden canvas); no way to clear a result; the seed was a Material outlined field with a floating "Automatic" label, twice as tall as the other fields, and "Fixed" could leave no number (a block default that is the engine's own seed) so the field showed an object and the run still drew a random one.
- New: Ctrl+V and Edit > Paste take a picture into the focused picture input, else the first (the command `edit.paste` is taken over while the App view is shown; a focused text field keeps Ctrl+V by the registry's rule); a picture dropped anywhere on the page goes there too (the well's own drop is above it). **Clear** (`appClear`), a flat 7u ViewButton with an x glyph, right-aligned above the output well, shown once there is something to clear or a run goes, disabled while it goes. The seed is one 7u row 1u apart: a plain well with the number (the DragNumberField's look: no outline, a 1px accent edge on focus, "New each run" in the label color while Random), a 7u dice button, a 22u drop-down of Fixed, Random, Increment.
- Why: a form field the owner pastes into is the common first move (Fitts: no pointer travel); the seed is the form's least important field and must not outweigh the prompt (Hick, Gestalt: one row); the mode is said by the mode control alone.
- Rules met: Floor (the dice, the drop-down and Clear are 7u/28px; the field 7u), Primary (Clear is flat: Run stays the one primary), Destructive (Clear loses nothing: the files stay on disk and in the results store, so no confirmation), Gaps (1u inside the seed row), Keys (Ctrl+V as the canvas's paste; Tab reaches the field, the dice, the mode).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): Clear returns the status to idle (also after a failed or cancelled run, and clears its error); the mode drop-down's words are shortened to Fixed, Random, Increment (the node face's longer sentences stay there); Fixed and Increment always hold a concrete number from the form's first showing, new if the workflow has none; Random to Fixed keeps the number the field last had (the form cannot know a run's own random seed).
- Not checked by a person: the look in a real window at 210 px, 1888 px wide; a paste with a real compositor's clipboard.

### 2026-11-24 App view in the layout of ComfyUI App Mode (#385, #389, epic #397; the owner's "let's try the comfy look")

- Reference: ComfyUI App Mode, read from docs.comfy.org and screenshots only (layout and behaviour; the frontend is GPL-3.0 and Desktop AGPL-3.0, so no code, CSS, icons or branding). Research: `workbench/fugue/apps-ui-research.md`. Parity is the floor; each deviation below.
- Old: a 210 px form at the start with a cut-off strip of four pictures, a plain bar for ranged numbers, Run pinned at the window's foot far from the inputs, the result alone in a large area with no controls and no zoom.
- New: `AppPage.qml` is a rail (`AppRail.qml`, 9u: Apps, Results and pictures, Graph; 7u flat icon buttons with tooltips), the stage (`AppStage.qml`: toolbar, the result in `CompareView` with wheel zoom about the pointer, drag to pan, Z, C and Home, the size and zoom line, Fit and 100%, the takes strip `AppTakes.qml`, 14u thumbnails) and the input panel at the end (a quarter of the window, 80u to 40%: title, two-line description, the form, Advanced as a quiet row, Run 9u tall and full width at the foot). Controls: `AppField` (7u well, hairline edge, accent 2px on focus), `AppSlider` (a thin track and handle with the value in a 14u field), `AppImageInput` (a 7u dropdown and folder button over a dashed 40u well with a hover remove button), choices as dropdowns.
- Why: Fitts (Run next to the inputs it runs, full width), Hick (one column, the quiet things quiet), Gestalt (a small dim label closer to its own control than to the one before it, 1/2u inside a field, 3u between fields), the owner's "blah" form and "media library is not great" and "the resulting image should be zoomable".
- Rules met: Floor (rail buttons, toolbar buttons, fields and dropdowns are 7u/28px; the compact Fit, 100%, Compare, Advanced and Add to launcher buttons are the 6u/24px `compact` ViewButton, on a quiet line), Primary (Run is the one accent fill; Cancel is a normal bordered button because Fugue keeps danger red for deletion; Clear loses nothing), Gaps (3u between the panel's fields, 1u between a label and its control and between the stage's rows), Learned place (File > Apps… and Run keep their places; Ctrl+Enter runs from any field), Posture (the page is a form and a viewer: fewer decisions; the dialogs and popovers are transient). Keys: Tab reaches rail, toolbar, picture, strip, form and Run; Z, C, Home, 0, 1, + and - act on the picture once it has the focus; arrows move in the takes strip and Enter shows one; Escape closes the dropdown, the Apps dialog and the library.
- Deviations from ComfyUI (each to confirm): the stage is at the start and the inputs at the end as in Comfy (Fugue's old form-first order is gone); no "Number of runs" stepper (a tab holds one run, queuing several is the run service's); Cancel is a bordered button, not filled red; the double click fits a zoomed picture and opens the large view at Fit (Comfy documents neither); Zoom, pan, compare with the input and Z/C are parity-plus; the rail has no build or share items; the takes are this session's (Comfy's survive in its queue panel; ours need the run history read back by workflow, #389); the picture dropdown groups Samples, Recent and This app's results, with Use sample; an app's own window changes to another app in place and the launcher entry and Open in Fugue follow the new app.
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): the double click behaviour above; Clear empties the stage but keeps the takes; a cancelled run with nothing made leaves no take; the Graph item calls `AppView::openInGraph()` (to follow the App | Graph toggle when it lands); Add to launcher is an icon with a tooltip, not a labelled button, to keep the panel's header quiet; a new tab with nothing in it lands on the shelf even when other tabs are open (`Workflows::currentIsPristine`), and Start blank still ends the shelf for the session.
- Not checked by a person: the look in a real window and in a real compositor; right-to-left was rendered offscreen only (the window's own left-edge strip overlaps the mirrored panel there, as it did before: Main.qml's margins).

### 2026-11-24 Selection Brush; the Brush paints masks only (#380, epic #216)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml`: the tool strip's `canvasViewTool-selbrush` (glyph `paintbrush`, 24px like its neighbours, in the lasso's chunk) and the command `canvas-view.tool-selection-brush` (key A); the live red of `canvasViewSelection` while it strokes, `canvasViewLiveRect` while a rectangle is dragged, the lasso's fill (`BrushPen.liveTint`); the layer mask's overlay tint (`canvasViewLayerMask`, now the accent, not red); Quick Mask's overlay on whenever Q is turned on. The model is `src/canvas/paint_target.h` (`PaintTarget`).
- Old: the Brush painted the selection when Quick Mask or the lasso was on and a layer's mask otherwise, so one tool painted two things by a mode that was easy to miss; the painted selection showed nothing until release (ants), and Quick Mask's red was behind a toggle that started off.
- New: three painters, each with one target. The Selection Brush paints the selection (Alt at the press subtracts, `[` and `]` size it) and shows the covered area live as 40% red; on release the ants take over. The Brush and Eraser paint the selected layer's mask only (Quick Mask excepted, below), with their own live trace in the mask's tint (accent) even when the Overlay is off. Quick Mask (Q) stays Photoshop's toggle: any brush paints the selection while it is on, and its red overlay is on at every entry (`\` hides it). The lasso and a dragged rectangle fill their area in the same red while the drag is on.
- Why: Photoshop Elements' Selection Brush and Photoshop's Quick Mask painting are the convention (References): "what the next action affects" (the selection, red) and "what a layer hides" (the mask) each get their own tool and colour. Photoshop's mask overlay is also red; Fugue differs on purpose, so the two never look alike (the owner asked for the split). Photoshop Elements' Selection Brush has Alt for subtract too; Alt+drag no longer sizes this brush (the other brushes keep it).
- Rules met: Floor (the strip button is the strip's 24px), Primary (none added), Destructive (nothing removed; every stroke is a selection undo step), Gaps (the strip's own), Keys (A had no owner in the registry; a tooltip names it), Glyphs (`paintbrush` added to the Lucide subset, means this tool only).

### 2026-11-24 Get ready sheet: one question for packs, trust, environments and models (#379)

- Control: `src/workflow/GetReadySheet.qml` (`getReadySheet`: `getReadyHeadline`, rows `getReadyPack-<id>` and `getReadyModel-<name>` with rows `GetReadyRow` (a kind glyph, name, size, a second line, a chevron: the whole row opens its details) in the groups Packs, Environments (`getReadyEnvironment-<id>`) and Models, `getReadyDestination` with `getReadyChange` (`getReadyDestinations` menu, `getReadyPickFolder`, `getReadyFolderDialog`), `getReadyLowSpace`, `getReadyMessage`, answers `getReadyCancel`, `getReadyAll`, `getReadyClose`, `getReadyAgain` ("Try again")); the banner's one button `getReady` ("Get ready…") in `WorkflowDialogs.qml`; Settings > Folders' "Download models into" (`download_folder`, `downloadFolder`).
- Old: a template that carried a pack asked in turn (the banner's Install, the pack's trust question, its setup question, each model's download), with an "Into <folder>" combo in the banner, visible only while a download was offered.
- New: one `Question` sheet lists the packs (install and trust), the environment each one's setup makes, and the models (file, size, host, licence, folder) behind "Details" rows, a **Models go into** row (the folder, the free space, the size needed, **Change…**), then **Install** (always that one word) and Cancel; a model whose type a pack of the list gives says "type from <pack>"; then one progress row each and **Stop**; success closes the sheet and shows the template's tab, a failure stops there and says what is left, **Try again** lists only that. The banner keeps its line and has the one button; the pack's separate trust question waits while that button is offered.
- Settings > Folders: **Wildcards** (`folderRow-wildcards`, last row, #556) shows the folder Dynamic Prompt reads `__name__` files from (`wildcardsFolder`, Default) and opens to a note only: it is fixed (the data folder's `wildcards/`; a pack's and the Default's are added by the engine), so it has no field, no Choose… and no Add. The glyph's tooltip is the full path.
- Settings > Folders: **Download models into** (`downloadFolder-<path>` choices, the quiet marking on the current one) sits under Extra model folders, lists Fugue's own folder and the model folders Fugue may write in, and sets the same `download_folder` key as the sheet's Change….
- Rules met: Primary (**Install** the one accent fill; Stop and Cancel plain), Destructive (nothing is removed; nothing happens before the yes), Gaps (3u between rows' chunks as the other questions, 2u between answers), Words ("Get ready…" with an ellipsis because a sheet follows; "Models go into"), Keyboard (Tab through the rows and the buttons; the sheet is a `Question`, Escape does not close it while it runs), Right-to-left (the content mirrors as every Question's).
- Follow-up (#418): a model row with a kept `.part` from an earlier stop added "partial, will resume" to its quiet subtitle line; replaced by the Paused status above (2026-11-27).
- 2026-11-24 (owner review): the sheet is laid out as a title, the one-line headline, then groups **Packs**, **Environments** and **Models** of `GetReadyRow`s (kind glyph, name, size at the right, a quiet second line with the licence or "type from <pack>", a chevron; the whole row opens its details, with the menu-row hover fill and the accent focus ring), cut at the end with a tooltip instead of in the middle, with only a vertical `FugueScrollBar` while the list overflows. **Download models to** is a labelled row with the folder, the free space and **Change model folder…** beside them; the pack warning is a quiet note above Cancel and Install; each row shows its state, a download its percentage and bar. The template details sheet uses the body size (`textSize`) for its text, `labelSize` for second lines and a larger section heading.
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): the sheet opens by itself when a template, package or workflow that is not ready is opened (the owner: one click is too many) and does nothing until the yes; Cancel closes it and the banner's **Get ready…** opens it again; **Change…** lists the folders that can take the models (the layout roots, a single-kind root only for its kind) and **Pick a folder…**; a low free space disables the yes until another folder is chosen; nothing is fetched or written before the yes: a git pack's row shows what the workflow records (source, pinned ref) and "details after the download", a folder pack is copied to a staging folder and installed from that copy; after the yes a git pack is cloned over https only (200 MB at most), checked against the id and major the workflow names, and a pack that declares network, file or GPU access asks once (**Allow** or **Skip**, that row alone); a picked folder and **Change…** are written to the settings at the yes, not before; each pack row also shows its source, permissions and setup command on one short line.

### 2026-11-23 Layers panel: groups as a tree (#278, epics #206 and #216)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasLayersPanel.qml`: the arrow `canvasLayerArrow-<position>` at the left of a group's row (flat compact `ViewButton`, 24px), the rows of an open group's layers (`canvasLayer-<position>` as before, indented), the Left and Right keys on `canvasLayerList`, the drop line `canvasLayerDropLine` (error color when the layers cannot land there), the folder glyph on a group inside a group, the row menu's top-level-only entries. The tree logic is `src/engine/canvas_layer_tree.h` (`LayerOutline`), the rows come from `CanvasViewModel::layers`.
- Old: a group was one row; its layers could only be reached by Ungroup, and `depth` was always 0.
- New: a group's row has an arrow (Photoshop's and Krita's disclosure triangle, Blender's outliner arrow) in a 6u column that exists only while the canvas has a group, so a flat canvas looks as before. A click opens or closes it; Right opens a closed group (again: into it), Left closes an open one (again: out to its group); Alt+click on an arrow opens or closes every group (Photoshop's Alt+click on the arrow). Groups start closed; the open state is kept by names, so it survives edits and renames. Layers inside are indented 3u a level. A hidden group dims what is inside it (the children's own eye keeps its own state and comes back with the group), a locked group protects its layers (the lock shows lit and dim on them, with the reason in its tip). A closing group takes the selection of the layers it hides, as Photoshop does. Edits work inside a group as at the top level: eye, lock, opacity, blend, rename, delete, duplicate, move, New layer (above the current one, in its group), Group and Ungroup; each is one revision. A Shift or Ctrl pick across groups is changed one group at a time (the panel says so). A drag moves layers within their group; a line elsewhere is red and the drop says why.
- Why: the owner's Photoshop model with Blender's interface (References); Photoshop and Krita show a group as a row with an arrow and indented children, and Blender's outliner dims the children of a hidden parent. The research is from their manuals as known; Photoshop's and Blender's pages could not be re-fetched in this session. Hick: groups are closed until wanted. Fitts: a 24px arrow at the row's edge, keys for the keyboard.
- Rules met: Floor (arrow 24px), Primary (none added), Destructive (nothing removed; every edit one revision with Undo), Gaps (1u inside a row as before), Learned place (the eye stays at the right edge, the thumbnails keep their place), Posture (dense: no extra rows or notices), Keys (Left and Right are the list's own, no command owns them). Deviations: the drag cannot move a layer into or out of a group yet (#278 stays open for it); Merge down, Flatten, Move and Free Transform, the mask brush, selections from a layer and Generate's layer read the top level only (the menu rows are disabled for a layer inside a group, the model says why); a layer inside a group cannot be shown alone.

### 2026-11-22 Candidate review removed from the Canvas view (#239, epic #216)

- Control: removed from `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml`: the new-candidate banner (`canvasViewNewResult`, `canvasViewNewBack`, `canvasViewNewAccept`, `canvasViewNewDrop`), the "N pending" chip (`canvasViewPending`), the bar's Accept and Drop (`canvasViewAccept`, `canvasViewDrop`), the Enter key as Accept, and the command `canvas-view.show-candidate` (View > Show candidate); from `src/canvas/CanvasLayersPanel.qml`: Accept and Drop in the read-only state (`canvasLayersAccept`, `canvasLayersDrop`; **Back to latest**, `canvasLayersGoHead`, stays); from `src/canvas/CanvasTaskBar.qml` and the panel: `decisionElsewhere`; from `Banner.qml`: `decision`; from Settings > General: **Show new candidates** (`canvas_show_new_results`, `canvasShowNewResults`).
- Old: a run could leave a candidate (Canvas Write's Keep as candidate, a saved value that beat the new default) and the view showed it over the head under a banner with Accept and Drop, a chip counted the waiting ones, and a setting turned the banner off.
- New: nothing to review. A run is a layer on the latest version (delete it, or Undo; the view goes along with "Layer <name> added" and Undo). The History panel's **All revisions** (kept) still lists every revision, the command line's candidates too, shown alone and read only with **Back to latest**.
- Why: the owner's decision (2026-11-22): candidate review is not a user concept (Photoshop's model: a run is a layer). Fewer decisions (Hick): the primary of the surface is no longer Accept, so the task bar's Generate is always the one accent fill.
- Rules met: Primary (Generate is the only accent control of the canvas surface; nothing turns it quiet), Destructive (no Drop; deleting a layer is the panel's, and Undo takes it back), Words (one word for a revision shown: Latest or its step). History dialog (File > Canvas history…) is kept and is to be retired later; its Accept and Drop stay for scripts and film teams.
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): the old Decisions rows below that name the banner, the chip, Accept, Drop or Show candidate describe what was removed here and stay as the record; **All revisions** stays as the way to see a candidate; an unplaceable Canvas Write fails the run with the plain words "The canvas changed size while this ran, so nothing was written. Run again." in the task bar's error line.

### 2026-11-22 A node of a missing pack: words and an Install button on its card (#264)

- Control: the error chip under a node card, `src/canvas/CanvasNode.qml` (`errorChip`): for an error of a type of a pack that is not installed, its line is "Needs the Remove Background pack" (the pack's display name) and, when the pack says where to get it, `installPackChip` ("Install…", 7u = 28px high, accent fill) on a second row at the line's left edge. The chip's tooltip is the whole error text (`errorDetail`), also for the other errors.
- Old: the chip showed the compiler's sentence, cut at four lines ("cut/find: Node cut/find has type io.github...remove-background, which is not installed and is not a gro..."), with no action.
- New: the chip names the pack in words, on a plain node and on a group instance whose inner node needs it; the button asks for the install as the banner's Install does (Settings > Packs, which asks before it installs and before it trusts), and stays after the banner is dismissed.
- Why: the person needs to act, not to read a type id (the cohesion line "no technical text around the work"); the button sits on the line that says what is missing (Fitts, one click), and the technical text is one hover away for a pro. The chip is the card's own surface for a failed node (4.7), so the action goes where the error already is.
