### 2026-11-21 player on a video or song node (#116)

- Control: `src/canvas/MediaView.qml`, in a node's picture slot (`CanvasNode.qml`, `mediaPlayer`) and in the large view (`MediaDialog.qml`, `mediaViewer`): the bar's `mediaPlay`, `mediaSeek`, `mediaMute` and `mediaExpand` (a video only), and the picture's double click.
- Old: none; a video or song result showed a label ("Video · 0:04").
- New: one bar, 7u = 28px tall, over the picture: play and pause first, the time, the seek rail (the rest of the width), then mute and expand. The large view has the same bar and the close button of `LargeViewDialog`, with no Fit and 100% (a player has no zoom).
- Why: play is the one thing wanted of a player, so it is first and in the same place on the face and the large view, as the dialog's primary; the rail is the widest target, since a seek is by pointing (Fitts); a press on the picture is not play, so it can select the node and a double click can open the large view, as for a picture. Space, Left, Right and M work once it has the focus.
- Rule: Floor (every button is 28px square; the rail's press area is the bar's whole 28px height, its rail 4px). Primary (play is the first control; the others are quiet). Gaps (one `u` between the time, rail and buttons). Learned place: no menu changed. Posture: on the canvas, a control that is frequent is raised to 28px, over the node's 24px floor; the dialog is transient and starts playing at once, so it asks no decision.

### 2026-11-21 hover magnifier setting (#123)

- Control: Settings > General, `src/settings/SettingsPanel.qml` "Magnify small nodes on hover" (`hoverMagnifier`), a `SettingsParts.Toggle` (`src/settings/SettingsParts.qml`), after Corner radius, before Language. Writes `hover_magnifier` in settings.json.
- Old: the magnifier (#74) always came up on a short hover far out.
- New: off by default, so a node never grows under the pointer unasked; on, it behaves as #74. The toggle's label reads On or Off beside the switch. Applies at once; turning it off closes an open magnifier.
- Why: the owner found a node growing on hover a surprise, not a help; a surprise costs more than the wire it saves, and the choice is one the user makes once.
- Rule: Floor (the whole row, switch and label, is the press target, 7u = 28px tall; the 22x12 switch is the same drawing as Providers' "Use" switch). Primary: none, a settings page has none. Posture: a setting, no frequent action. The toggle takes Tab focus; Space or Enter flips it.

### 2026-11-21 Bars and panels menu, focus mode and hideable bars (#129)

- Control: a submenu **Bars and panels** at the end of the View menu, then **Focus mode** (H) (`src/menu/menus.cpp`, `barsMenu()`), and the same submenu in the canvas's context menu. (A top Window menu was built first and dropped: its title added 4 items to the window's budget.) Rows, each a checked command while its bar is shown: Menu bar, Workflow tabs, Run toolbar, Selection toolbar, Group breadcrumb, Run status, Zoom readout (`view.toggle-*`); then Node library, Workflows, Run queue (`panel.*`, moved from View); then Focus mode (H) and Reset bars and panels. The hint pill `chromeHint` (`Main.qml`), a line at the bottom for 4 s after the menu bar is hidden or focus mode starts.
- Old: View held the three panel rows and the run toolbar toggle; no other bar could be hidden.
- New: as above. The View menu keeps fit, zoom and links.
- Why: a list of every bar with a check is recognition, not recall (Hick), as Photoshop's Window menu; the chunks are the bars, the panels, then the two whole-window actions, the gap between chunks being a separator. The menu bar can hide itself, so the way back is shown when it goes (the pill names F10) and the canvas's menu carries the same list; F10 shows a hidden bar before it opens it.
- Rule: Learned place (the panel rows keep their order and keys, N, W, Alt+Q; they moved from View to the menu that is now for bars; the existing View rows keep theirs). Floor (menu rows keep the menus' row height; the pill is not a target). Gaps (separators between the three chunks, the menus' own). Destructive: not applicable, and hiding is reversible by the same row, F10, H or Reset. Posture: transient menu rows, no decision beyond a check. Primary: none added.

### 2026-11-21 models to download in the model picker (#134)

- Control: the model menu of any resource input (`src/canvas/widgets/WidgetMenu.qml` via `InputWidget.qml`, `modelEntries()`): after the files, a heading **Download** and one row per catalog model not on disk (title, size at the right, a second line with scale, architecture, license and what it is for). Picking one opens a question (`WidgetPicker.qml`, `downloadAsk`, a `Question`): title "Download <name>?", its description, License (the error color and "not for commercial use" when the license forbids it), Made by, Size, From (host), Saved in (folder), a line saying the file is kept only if its SHA-256 matches, then `downloadAskLicenseLink` (flat, "Read the license", at the left), `downloadAskCancel` and `downloadAskOk` ("Download", the one highlighted button, last at the right).
- Old: a model menu listed files and provider models; a missing model was fetched only from a workflow's banner or a template card, and a Load Upscale Model had nothing to list.
- New: as above. The search field also matches the architecture, license, author and tags of the downloadable rows (`keywords`). The download goes on the window's one download line, so the banner shows its progress and Cancel; when the file has arrived and its SHA-256 matched, the input holds it.
- Why: choosing a model and getting it is one act, so it happens where the model is chosen (Hick: no trip to a model library); a download is large and has a license, so it is asked once, with what decides it in view (spec 6.7: nothing silent), and never by Enter on a filtered list alone (Enter picks the row and opens the question; the question's Enter is not bound, the highlighted button is the answer).
- Rule: Learned place (the menu keeps its order: Any ready model, provider models, files, then Download, then Type a name…). Floor (menu rows are the menus' row height; the question's buttons are Material's 48px as the group questions'). Primary (Download is the question's one highlighted button; Cancel before it, as the other questions have them). Destructive: none, nothing is deleted and Cancel fetches nothing. Gaps (3u between the question's chunks, 1u inside the facts grid, 2u between the buttons). Posture: the menu is transient and keeps its density; the question is a transient dialog with one decision.

### 2026-11-21 move a node to a newer major version (#118)

- Control: the node's chip `updateChip` (`CanvasNode.qml`, the one a group shows as "Update to 1.1.0"), now also "Move to 2.1.0" on a node that runs on an older installed major of its type (`NewerMajor` role); the Node menu's and the node menu's `node.move-version` ("Move to newer version…", shown only for one selected node that has a newer major); and the question `groupUpdateAsk` (`GroupDialogs.qml`, `major` set), whose highlighted button reads "Move".
- Old: the offer was the compile warning `node_newer_major` and the CLI; moving meant editing the node's `version` in the file.
- New: as above. The chip is the group chip's place (right end, above the node's header), size and look; a click or the command asks, naming the inputs and outputs that come and go (or that they stay the same), then Move rewrites the version, one undo step. A node never has both chips (a group has its library update, a node its newer major).
- Why: it is the same act as a group's update, so it is asked the same way (recognition, Hick); the question comes first because moving to a new major can change results (spec 3.6, 6: nothing changes silently), and Cancel before Move leaves the file as it was.
- Rule: Floor (the chip is the group update chip's 16px by text width, an existing exception on the canvas, and the command is the keyboard way; the question's buttons are the group questions'). Primary (Move is the question's one highlighted button, last at the right, Cancel before it). Destructive: none, undo reverses the move. Gaps (the question's own: 3u between chunks). Learned place (the Node menu keeps its order; the new row is last, after the group rows, and shows dimmed in the bar; in the node menu it follows Update group). Posture: the chip is canvas furniture, shown only while there is something to do; the question is a transient dialog with one decision.

- Old: the button offered only the `requires.packs` source of a missing pack; the card said "Packs needed".

- Rule: Floor, Primary, Destructive, Gaps, Learned place and Posture: unchanged, the button and the card keep their size, place and order; only the text differs.

### 2026-11-21 media browser and the workflows panel's Results view (#133)

- Controls: `src/panels/WorkflowsPanel.qml` (`workflowsView-files`, `workflowsView-results`, `workflowsResultsRefresh`); `src/media/MediaBrowser.qml` (`mediaMode-grid|filmstrip|single`, `mediaInfo`, `mediaAction-open|folder|copy`), the grid cells `MediaThumb.qml` (`mediaCell-<n>`), the filmstrip cells and the large item `MediaSingle.qml`.
- Old: the workflows panel was the file lists only.
- New: a row of switch buttons at the top (Files, Results, then Refresh while Results shows), under it the browser's bar (Grid, Filmstrip, Single, Info, the count), the grid or the large item, and the actions in the last row. Every button is a `ViewButton`, 7u = 28px tall. A grid cell is 24u = 96px square, a filmstrip cell 16u = 64px, both well above the floor.
- Rules: Floor (all targets 28px or more). Gaps (1u inside each row; the rows are the chunks, separated by 1u as the panel's lists are, the switch row above the list by 2u). Primary: none on this surface; browsing has no main action, and Open is the first of the three actions on the current item, in the order of how often a file is wanted. Destructive: none (delete is a follow-up and will be its own chunk). Posture: a docked panel, so the canvas's density.
- Keys: the browser takes Left, Right, Up, Down, PageUp, PageDown, Home, End, Enter, Escape and I through `onShortcutOverride`, so the window's own shortcuts (Home fits the view) do not take them while it has the focus; Escape goes back to the grid, then closes the panel.
- Not checked by a person: the layout at the panel's narrowest width (200px: the buttons wrap) and in a light theme.

### 2026-11-21 fit on open, clear of the run toolbar (#162)

- Control: none added. `FugueCanvas.qml` `fit()` (View > Fit, `view.fit`) and the same fit when a workflow with no saved view is put in; `floatingBar` is the run toolbar's box, set by `Main.qml`.
- Old: a template opened at 100%, nodes cut off at the edge and under the run toolbar; Fit used the whole canvas, so the bar still covered the top row.
- New: the nodes fill three quarters of the canvas without the band the bar covers (its box and 2u, on the top or bottom edge, whichever it is on); the canvas already starts after the 44px tab strip and a docked panel. A saved view is kept.
- Why: the first look at a workflow is recognition (Hick): the whole graph is in view and none of it is behind a control (Fitts: a node under the bar cannot be reached by the pointer).
- Rule: no new control, so Floor, Primary, Gaps and Destructive do not apply. Learned place: Fit's key and menu entry are unchanged. Posture: the canvas keeps its density.

### 2026-11-21 scroll bars on every scrolling surface

- Control: `src/canvas/FugueScrollBar.qml` (`fugueScrollBar`), on the lists and panels named in canvas-look 16.1; replaces the plain Material `ScrollBar {}`, the AlwaysOn-when-long copies and the CommandMenu's ScrollIndicator. The widget menu's list also takes Home and End when it has the keys (a menu with no search field).
- Old: the model picker, the other widget menus, the command menus, the multiline editor, the run queue, the group editor, the media details and the filmstrip scrolled with no visible bar; some panels had one in the style's default look, hidden until moved.
- New: one themed bar, visible whenever the content overflows, a draggable thumb of at least 32px, a press on the track pages.
- Why: a long list (hundreds of model files) was found by wheel or arrow keys only; a bar shows where the person is and drags to the far end at once (Fitts: the thumb is a target as long as the list is long).
- Rule: Floor, with an exception: the bar is a 10px gutter along the whole edge of its view, hit along its length, a 24px circle on it would cover the rows it borders; the wheel and keys do the same scrolling. Reversed 2026-11-21: the first version let the bar lie over the content and took no width (only the widget menu padded for it), which cut off the last key chip of every row of the Shortcuts overlay and put the library's thumb under the panel's width grip, so it could not be dragged. The bar now reserves its gutter: `inset` (its breadth while it shows), and each host pads its content by it; the side panel's body stops short of the grip. Tests: `tests/scroll_checks.h` (no text or image under the bar, wheel and thumb drag scroll). Posture: no new decision, nothing to choose.

### 2026-11-21 the wheel on every scrolling surface, and one themed tooltip

- Control: the wheel handler inside `src/canvas/FugueScrollBar.qml` (every view that has the bar scrolls by it) and `src/canvas/ToolTipStyle.qml` (made once by `Main.qml`; the one look of every `ToolTip.text` / `ToolTip.visible` in the window), with `src/canvas/TipGate.qml` on the node library's and the workflows panel's lists.
- Old: the wheel did not scroll a list in the real window. On the owner's Wayland session an ordinary mouse wheel is a device of type TouchPad with no scroll phase (angle 120, pixel 15 per notch): the library's `WheelHandler` took the default devices and never heard it, and a Flickable's own wheel handling did not move for it either (tests/scroll_checks.h `waylandWheelAt` reproduces it). A tooltip was as wide as its text: a node's description was one 1500px line above the row, delayed 600ms, and a tooltip fading for 500ms under a pointer that kept scrolling took the wheel events.
- New: the bar's handler scrolls its view for a Mouse or a TouchPad device: a notch is 60px, a touchpad (told by its scroll phase alone, as the canvas does) moves by its pixels; the touchpad path and the thumb drag are unchanged. Every tooltip is wrapped at 80u (320px at the default text size, about 45 characters), never wider than the window less its margins, shown below its control with the left edges aligned, after 700ms (`look.tooltipDelay`; each site sets `ToolTip.delay` to it), and it goes at once (no fade out). The library's and workflows lists hide a row's tooltip when the list scrolls, until the pointer moves.
- Why: Fitts and steering: a tooltip over what the person is aiming at, or lying where the scrolled row was, is an obstacle in the path; a long line is not read (the eye returns to the line start: lines of 40 to 50 characters read best), and a short delay shows it by a deliberate rest, not on the way across (Hick: the pointer passing over twelve rows should not open twelve). Below, not beside: the pointer comes from above or the side, the hovered row stays readable, and the rows that tooltip covers are the ones not being read.
- Rule: Floor (no new control; the tooltips are not targets). Primary, Destructive, Gaps (1u between the control and its tooltip), Learned place (desktop tooltips: Qt, GNOME and KDE show them below the control after about 500 to 1000ms), Posture: transient, never takes the pointer's events. Tests: `theWheelScrollsWhileARowTooltipShows` and `aRowTooltipIsWrappedAndInsideTheWindow` (tests/panels/test_side_panels_window.cpp), `scrollsByWheelAndThumb` for every page of the settings and every menu. The masking editor's own tooltip (`MaskEditor.qml`) keeps its 600ms until the masking work lands.

### 2026-11-21 where a model lives: badges on template cards and model rows, Ready to run (#164)

- Control: `src/canvas/PlaceBadge.qml` (`templatePlaceLocal-<id>`, `templatePlaceHost-<id>`, `widgetMenuPlace`), a 16px `monitor` (this machine) or `server` (a paired host) glyph with a tooltip, on a pill at the lower left of each Templates card's preview (`templatePlaces-<id>`) and left of the detail text of each model menu row; the **Ready to run** chip (`templatesReadyOnly`, 7u tall, a toggle: a check and the accent edge when on) right of the Templates search field.
- Old: a card said only what it lacked ("Models missing"); a model row said where it lived only by the heading it sat under, and an offline host's models read "not running".
- New: as above. A template with models on both places shows both badges; none when none are found (the "Models missing" line stays). An offline host's badge and row are dimmed, the row says "host offline". With the chip on, only templates whose models are all somewhere show, and within a category the ready ones come first.
- Why: where a model lives decides whether a template can run now, and a glyph is read faster than a line (recognition, not recall: Hick); the tooltip carries the host's name so the glyph is not the only signal; the pill keeps the card the same height, so nothing reflows when the hosts answer after the dialog opens.
- Rule: Floor (the badges are informational, they take no press, so no 28px floor; the chip is 7u = 28px tall and the whole chip is the press target). Primary (none: the chip is a toggle, the dialog's action is opening a card). Destructive (none). Gaps (1u between badges; 2u between the search field and the chip). Learned place (the search field keeps its place at the top; the chip sits on the search's row at the right, as ComfyUI's filters sit by its search). Posture (a dialog read at arm's length: the glyphs are 16px, the same as the other row glyphs; the tooltip names the host).

### 2026-11-22 queue history rows name a run and open the media browser (#133)

- Control: the finished rows of the run queue panel (`RunQueuePanel.qml`): a `historyLead-<run>` thumbnail (7u = 28px square, `MediaThumb` with `compact`: no name, no handlers) after the outcome glyph, the name line (prompt, else the first output's file name, else the workflow's name) and a detail line that now ends with the workflow's name; `historyResults-<run>` ("Show its results") and the row click open the workflows panel's Results view on that run; there `workflowsResultsAllRuns` ("All runs") widens it. The panel is 104u wide (was 96u) for the thumbnail.
- Old: every row read "workflow" and showing results meant only the canvas, and only for the workflow of the tab shown.
- New: as above; the canvas still shows the results as before, and the old "Open the workflow..." notice shows only in a window with no side panels.
- Why: recognition over recall (a picture and a prompt tell runs apart where a shared name cannot); the press and its place do not change, so no relearning (Learned place).
- Rule: Floor (the thumbnail is not a target: the whole row, 9u = 36px tall, is). Primary (the row click). Destructive (none). Gaps (unchanged). Posture: pointer, as before.

### 2026-11-25 video and sound thumbnails (#133)

- Control: `MediaThumb.qml` (grid cells `mediaCell-<n>`, filmstrip cells, queue `historyLead-<run>`): the picture is now a frame of a video or the waveform of a sound, with a small `play` or `volume-2` mark in the lower right corner (10px compact, 14px otherwise); no new control, no new target.
- Old: a video or a sound showed a glyph and its name in every cell.
- New: the cell shows the frame (taken 0.5 s in) or waveform once it is made; the glyph and name stay while a file cannot be read (broken file, no ffmpeg) and the cell shows only its well while the picture is loading, so no glyph flashes under a thumbnail.
- Why: recognition over recall (a frame tells clips apart where a file name cannot); the mark keeps a video from being taken for a still.
- Rule: Floor (unchanged: the mark is not a target, the cell is). Primary, Destructive, Gaps, Posture: unchanged.

### 2026-11-27 get new templates (#699)

- Control: Settings > General, `src/settings/SettingsPanel.qml` "Get new templates" (`fetchTemplates`), a `SettingsParts.Toggle`, after Reopen tabs at start, before Language. Writes `fetch_templates` in settings.json. Its note ends with the "Last checked" time or the last error. The window gains no other control: the templates arrive on their own.
- Old: a new or fixed template reached a person only with a Fugue release.
- New: Fugue fetches the changed template files on its own, at most every 6 hours; the switch turns it off and applies at once; Browse templates lists new templates when the fetch ends.
- Why: no steps to repeat (it just works), a choice made once so it is a setting, and a visible state ("Last checked") so a silent failure can be seen.

- Control: Settings > General, `src/settings/SettingsPanel.qml` "Reopen tabs at start" (`restoreTabs`), a `SettingsParts.Toggle`, after Magnify small nodes on hover, before Language. Writes `restore_tabs` in settings.json. The window itself gains no control: the tabs come back on their own.
- Old: every start opened one blank workflow.
- New: the saved workflows open again in their order with the same one shown; the switch turns it off and applies at once. A banner line names tabs whose files are gone.
- Why: work resumes where it stopped (recognition over recall, no steps to repeat); a choice made once, so it is a setting and not a prompt at each start.

### 2026-11-22 the Canvas view's menu bar (#232 slice B)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasMenuBar.qml` (`canvasMenuBar`, titles `canvasMenuTitle-<File|Edit|Select|Layer|View>`), the first row of `CanvasView.qml`, above the tools row; its popup is the window's own `CommandMenu` (`canvasMenuDropdown`, rows `menuItem-<command>`) on the registry's `canvas-view` tree (`menus.cpp`, `CommandRegistry::menu`). The questions of File are `CanvasFileDialogs.qml` (`canvasRenameAsk`, `canvasClearAsk`, `canvasDeleteAsk`, each a `Question`). Research: [canvas-menus-research.md](canvas-menus-research.md).
- Old: History, Layers, Show candidate, Compare and Export… were buttons in the view's top bar, Clear selection a button of the Quick Mask options, and a rectangle could not be removed at all.
- New: every one of those is a row of a menu (File: New canvas…, Open canvas…, Import picture…, Export ▸, Rename, Duplicate, Clear…, Delete…, Close; Edit: Undo, Redo; Select: Select all Ctrl+A, Deselect Ctrl+D, Inverse Ctrl+Shift+I, Quick Mask, Use selection; Layer: the mask rows; View: Fit, 100%, Zoom, History, Layers, Compare, Show candidate, Advanced details, Snap, Tools ▸). The bar keeps what a pointer needs often: the canvas picker, the pending chip, the compare modes while Compare is on, Accept and Drop, Fit and 100%, the host's close; the options bar keeps each tool's own settings (Snap, Zoom in or out, the brush's, Overlay, Use selection). A row of a feature not built yet is greyed, no command, with the tooltip "Not built yet (#N)". Escape clears the selection when nothing else wants it (no drag, no run); the next Escape is the dialog's close.
- Why: recognition over recall (one place lists everything the view can do, with its key beside it: Hick); the pointer's frequent controls stay a click away (Fitts); a greyed row says what is coming and where it is tracked, so a person is not left looking for it.
- Rule: Floor (titles are 6u = 24px compact buttons inside a 6u bar, rows are 7u = 28px as the window's menus). Primary (none: a menu is not a primary; Accept stays the one). Destructive (Delete canvas… and Clear canvas… ask first with Keep highlighted and focused, the destructive answer last in the danger tint after the wider gap; the Delete row is in the error color). Gaps (the bar 1u over the tools row). Posture: pointer first, keys for the frequent ones; no Alt+letter (the window's own bar owns those letters). Deviations, each to confirm: a view's keys that it handles by hand (Home, 0, 1, + and -) are shown in the menu as text and are not registered twice; Undo and Redo stay enabled outside Quick Mask (they act on the document's undo stack, not only the canvas's).

- Control: `mediaVolume` in `MediaView.qml`, a 4px rail with the accent fill between the seek track and the mute button; hidden in a bar narrower than 70u (a node's face), where the mute button's wheel and Up and Down still work.
- Old: mute was the only control; a loud song could not be turned down.
- New: click or drag the rail, the wheel over it or over the mute button (5%), Up and Down once the player has the focus (10%); any volume above zero unmutes; the mute tooltip says the percent. It is per player and not kept (no settings store for it yet).
- Why: the person who plays a result next to others needs it lower (Fitts: the rail is as high as the 28px bar; recognition: the fill shows the level).
- Rule: Floor (the rail's hit area is the 28px bar's height). Primary (none). Destructive (none). Gaps (unchanged). Posture: pointer, and keys.

### 2026-11-22 the window bar is global, each area's header has its view's menus (#262, slices 1 and 2)

- Control: the window bar (`menuBar`): File, Edit, Run, Window, Help; the Graph's area header (`areaHeader-<id>`, with `areaMenus-<id>` holding `canvasMenuBar` in its `graph` form: titles `canvasMenuTitle-View|Select|Add|Node`, and `canvasMenuButton`, the folded Menu button); `graphHeaderHost`, the same header above the canvas while the window has one area.
- Old: the bar held every menu (File, Edit, View, Node, Run, Help), the default window had no header, and the Graph's commands were in the window's bar; Bars and panels and Areas were submenus of View.
- New (Blender's model): the bar is the window's (a Window menu now lists every bar and panel, focus mode and the areas); each area's header is its view-type menu, then its view's own menus (the Graph's View, Select, Add, Node; the Canvas view's Canvas, Edit, Select, Layer and View stay in the view, until slice 3), then split and close at the right. Close is off while there is one area. In a header too narrow for the titles (they need more than the room between the view-type menu and the splits) they fold into one Menu button that opens them as submenus. Pressing along the titles while one is open moves to the next, as in the window bar.
- Why: recognition over recall (a view's commands sit with that view: Hick); one place per command, so no command at two levels; the default window and a split window have the same chrome (consistency). Header height stays 7u (28px).
- Rule: Floor (titles are 6u compact buttons in a 7u header). Primary (none). Destructive (none). Gaps (unchanged). Posture: pointer, and the window bar's Alt+letter (E, F, H, R, W).
- Not done in this change: the workflow tabs still have their own row under the window bar (Blender has them in the bar's row), and the Canvas view's menus are in a row inside the view, not in its area's header. (Both are done in the next entry.)

### 2026-11-23 the workflow tabs in the window bar's row, the Canvas view's menus in its area header (#262, slice 3)

- Control: the window bar (`menuBar`, 7u) now holds, left to right: File, Edit, Run, Window, Help; the workflow tabs (`workflowTabs`, `src/workflow/WorkflowTabs.qml`: `tab-<i>`, `closeTab-<i>`, `newTab`, as before); the project chip (`projectChip`); Close (`closeApp`). The Canvas view's area header (`areaHeader-<id>`) holds its view-type menu, then `areaMenus-<id>` with `canvasHeaderMenuBar` (titles `canvasHeaderMenuTitle-<Canvas|Edit|Select|Layer|View>`, the folded `canvasHeaderMenuButton`), then split and close. The File > Canvas… dialog has no header, so there the view keeps its own bar inside it (`canvasMenuBar`, `canvasMenuTitle-*`, as before): `CanvasView.ownMenus` is on in the dialog and off in an area. Which menus a view type's header holds, and the tab strip's widths and scroll, are rules of the menu model (`menus.h` `viewMenus`, `tabWidth`, `tabScroll`; QML reads them through `MenuLayout`), tested without a window (`tests/commands/test_menu_layout.cpp`).
- Old: the tabs were a row of their own, 9u (36px) high, under the window bar, and the Canvas view's menus a row inside the view, so the window bar's row and the headers were split from where Blender puts them.
- New (Blender: workspace tabs in the top bar; Photoshop: document tabs beside the application bar on Windows and Linux): the tabs take the room between the menus and the project chip, 2u clear of each, 6u (24px) tall inside the 7u bar (a tab is 36u = 144px at most, 16u = 64px at least; the New button 7u). They keep their colors, unsaved dot, close button and right-click menu; the strip shows only from the second workflow on, as before. When they do not fit they share the room down to the least width, then the strip scrolls (the wheel over it), and the current tab is kept whole in view (switching to a tab by key or from the Workflows panel scrolls to it). The Canvas view's menus are its area header's, as the Graph's are; a press on a title opens the menu on the view of that header's area and its keys are live while it is open, as the view's own bar did.
- Alt letters: only the window bar's five (F, E, R, W, H). No area header has any, so Alt+E is the window's Edit whichever area is active and the Canvas header's Edit and Select, which share letters with the window's, never fight it (Blender has none in its headers either; Photoshop's single bar has them because it has one set of menus). F10 opens the window bar's first menu. A header's menus open with the pointer, and Left and Right move along the open ones.
- Hiding the window bar (View > Bars and panels > Menu bar) auto-hides the tabs with it, since they are in its row; they slide in with it (the hot edge, F10, an Alt letter). The separate Workflow tabs check (`view.toggle-tabs`) still removes the tabs from a shown bar. Workflows can also be switched from the Workflows panel while the bar is out.
- Why: one top row for what is global (the window's menus and its documents: Fitts and fewer rows of chrome, 36px less height in every window with two workflows), the commands of a view with that view's header (recognition: Hick), one place per command. The window's item count falls by two.
- Rule: Floor (a tab 24px tall inside the 28px bar, its close button 5u = 20px: a deviation to confirm, it was 24px in a 36px tab; the New button 28px wide; titles stay 24px compact buttons). Primary (none). Destructive (the close button of a tab is as before: it asks about unsaved content through the window's question). Gaps (menus and tabs 2u apart, the chip 2u from the tabs). Posture: pointer first; keys as before. Right to left mirrors the strip and the scroll.

### 2026-11-23 Sampler preview: the picture while it samples (#297)

- Control: Settings > General, `src/settings/SettingsPanel.qml` "Sampler preview" (`stepPreview`), a `Choices` of three (Off, Fast, Full picture), after Node pictures, before Reopen tabs at start. Writes `step_preview` in settings.json.
- Old: a Sampler card showed nothing until its image was done.
- New: while a local helper samples, the card shows the image changing, about eight times a run. Fast (the default) is a rough picture that costs almost nothing; Full picture is the model's decoder, clearer and a little slower. The note under the row says which is on and that it is for a model on this machine.
- Why: a long run says what it is making (feedback while waiting), and the cost is a taste, so a setting inherited by projects. No control on the Sampler itself: the choice is about the machine, not the node.
- Rule: Floor (the 28px `Choices` buttons are the existing ones). Primary (the three buttons, one choice). Destructive (none). Gaps (unchanged). Posture: pointer, once.

### 2026-11-23 the live frame shows in every picture mode

- Control: none new; the Sampler preview and Node pictures settings behave together (`CanvasRuns::show`, `RunThread::withThumbnails`).
- Old: in Sinks and inputs only (or Smart, where a node's picture is dropped) a Sampler's live frame never showed: its picture slot was hidden and the frame's thumbnail was not even made.
- New: while a node (or, on a group card, a node inside it) runs and sends live frames, its card shows the frame whatever the mode says. When the node ends, the picture goes again unless the mode keeps it, so the card is tall during the run and short after (superseded by the overlay below). A node whose own override is Hide picture shows no frame.
- Why: feedback while waiting is the point of the preview (the mode is about the finished graph, a frame is about now); the override is the owner's explicit word on one node and wins, as it does over the mode. The card growing while a run is live is expected, the way a card grows when a result arrives in Smart mode.
- Rule: Floor (none). Primary (none). Destructive (none). Gaps (unchanged). Posture: none. Not seen in a real window.

### 2026-11-23 the live frame is an overlay where the picture is hidden

- Control: none new; `src/canvas/LiveFrame.qml`, drawn by `FugueCanvas.qml` in the world over the cards (`CanvasRuns::show` sends `liveFrame`, `liveAspect`).
- Old: where the mode hides a node's picture, the card gained a picture slot at the first live frame and lost it at the end, so the card grew and shrank and its wires rerouted twice a run, and it could cover the nodes below.
- New: the frame is a separate item directly under the card, as wide as the card, its height from the frame's aspect (0.5 to 1 of the width, as a card's picture), on the card surface with a hairline and the picture's rounded well. It is above every card, ignores the pointer (clicks reach what is under it), follows pan and zoom with the world and fades out in 200 ms when the node stops running or its result comes. A group's card shows its inside's frame the same way. Where the card shows its picture (Smart mode for a Sampler, or a sink) the frame stays in the card and there is no overlay; Hide picture shows nothing. Far out, the hover magnifier's copy of a card has no overlay.
- Why: a live frame never changes layout (no reflow, no wires moving, nothing covered for good); the frame is about now and the card about the graph. Under the card, not beside it, because a card's neighbours sit to its left and right along the wires, and the space below is the one a wire never crosses.
- Rule: Floor (none). Primary (none). Destructive (none). Gaps (1u under the card). Posture: none (non-interactive). Not seen in a real window.

### 2026-11-22 node pictures: mode and per-node override (#263)

- Control: Settings > General, `src/settings/SettingsPanel.qml` "Node pictures" (`pictureMode`), a `Choices` of three (Smart, Sinks and inputs only, All), after Magnify small nodes on hover, before Reopen tabs at start; the Graph header's View > Picture submenu (`view.picture-smart`, `view.picture-sinks`, `view.picture-all`, one checked); `node.picture-show`, `node.picture-hide` and `node.picture-default` ("Show picture", "Hide picture", "Default picture") in the Graph header's Node menu and the node's right-click menu, after Pin, left out where they would change nothing. Writes `picture_mode` in settings.json and `layout.nodes.<id>.picture`.
- Old: every node with a result showed its picture, so a chain of transforms repeated one thumbnail down the graph, and nothing said how to stop it.
- New: Smart (the default) keeps the picture on what makes, loads or ends (Load Image, Generate, Sampler, decodes, Preview, Save, Compare) and drops it from a node that only changes the one before (Resize, Blur, Flip, masks); the node is shorter and its thumbnail is not made. A node's own choice wins over the mode.
- Why: less to read and to scan past (Hick; a repeated picture is noise) and a way out that is where the eye already is (the node's menu, recognition over recall); the mode is a taste, so a setting inherited by projects and also in the View menu; the override is about one node, so it is in the workflow file. The menu items are plain commands, the check mark in View > Picture says which mode is on.
- Group cards: a group instance is a result node that shows a picture like a Load Image: before a run, the image its face's file input names (a group that exposes a Load Image's file; Sinks and inputs only counts it as a Load node), after a run the image its first image output makes. Hide and Show picture work on it as on any node. (Found 2026-11-23: a group card never had a picture source of its own until then.)
- Rule: Floor (menu rows and the 28px `Choices` buttons are the existing ones). Primary (the mode's three buttons, one choice). Destructive (none; nothing is deleted, a hidden picture comes back with a run, undo restores the override). Gaps (unchanged). Posture: pointer, once; the keymap can bind the commands.
- Not done: a control in a node's Advanced section (its rows are the type's inputs; the override is not one).

### 2026-11-22 file pickers ask the desktop portal (`FilePicker`)

- Control: `src/menu/FilePicker.qml`, the one file and folder picker (`openDialog`, `saveDialog`, `exportDialog`, `compiledDialog`, `imageSaveDialog`, `newCanvasFileDialog`, `canvasHistoryExportDialog`, `canvasViewExportDialog`, `canvasHistoryImportDialog`, `canvasViewImportDialog`, `canvasPictureDialog`, `widgetFileDialog`, `widgetFolderDialog`, `settingsFolderDialog`, `projectFolderDialog` keep their names), over `PortalFileDialog` (`src/menu/portal_file_dialog.*`).
- Old: every picker was Qt's `FileDialog`/`FolderDialog`. Omarchy's platform theme (`QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=qtengine`) has no native file dialog, so Qt showed its own QML one in a window of its own, with a Windows-like title bar, the Material look and dark text on a dark side bar; the comments said the desktop portal's was used, which was not so.
- New: File > Open, Save as, Export and every other picker asks `org.freedesktop.portal.FileChooser` (OpenFile, SaveFile) on the session bus, so the desktop's own file-picker backend draws it (Omarchy's `flea`), with the name filters, the folder and a suggested name. Qt's dialog is the fallback when the portal is not on the bus or its call fails, and it is now an item of the window (`popupType: Popup.Item`), so it takes the window's Material look from `App.theme` and reads on its surface: no system title bar, no second window.
- Why: consistency (one picker for the desktop's apps; recognition over recall: the person's bookmarks, recents and the folder they last used are there), and a fallback that is never worse than before.
- Rule: Floor (none of ours: the portal draws it). Primary (none). Destructive (the save picker asks about replacing a file, as before; Fugue still refuses to replace a canvas picture silently). Gaps (n/a). Posture: pointer and keys, the desktop's.
- Not done: the portal's picker has no parent window handle (`parent_window` is empty; the xdg-foreign handle is not exported), so a compositor may not stack it over the window as a transient. Not seen live on the owner's desktop yet (the tests have no session bus).

### 2026-11-22 the active canvas: a Canvas Region's empty canvas (#246, epic #206)

- Control: the `canvas` widget's menu and face (`src/canvas/widgets/InputWidget.qml`, `canvasMenu.js`), and the mismatch banner of `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml` (`canvasViewUseActiveForNode`). The view reports its canvas to `App.activeCanvas`.
- Old: an empty canvas read "Choose a canvas" and the first edit in the view wrote the shown canvas into the node, so a node never followed the view afterwards; a node on another canvas than the view showed the notice for any node.
- New: an empty canvas is the **Active canvas**: the face says "Active canvas" (dimmed, as a default), and the menu's first row, "Active canvas" (checked while empty, "follows the Canvas view" at the right, a note saying what it does), is the way back in one click. A canvas picked from the list pins the node: the face reads "<name> · pinned". The Canvas view's notice for a node on another canvas appears for pinned nodes only and has a third button, "Use the active canvas", after Show and Use this canvas. Drawing a rectangle or using a selection on an unpinned node writes only the rectangle; its canvas stays empty. Pick region… is enabled for an empty canvas too.
- Why: the picture you work on should be the one the model works on (Photoshop's Generative Fill has no such choice to make). Default first (Hick: no choice for the common case); pinning stays a visible, reversible choice (the "pinned" word names it, one click undoes it). The notice is for the exception only.
- Rules met: Floor (rows 24px, banner buttons 28px as the other two), Primary (no new accent fill), Gaps (the new row heads the list, 0 gap to the canvases: it is one list; the actions chunk is unchanged under its hairline).
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): "Active canvas" is also the face of a node in a root with no canvas at all (the run then says "Choose a canvas" in words); the active canvas is reported when a view opens, shows another canvas, or the picture takes focus (a press on it); a canvas deleted while active falls back to the newest.

### 2026-11-23 canvas export dialogs: quality and scale, layers to files (#247, #248)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasExportDialog.qml` (`canvasExportDialog`, a `Question`; `canvasExportTitle`, `canvasExportPreview`, `canvasExportFormat-<png|jpg|webp|ora>`, `canvasExportLossless`, `canvasExportQuality`, `canvasExportScale-<25|50|100|200>`, `canvasExportWidth`, `canvasExportHeight`, `canvasExportBackground-<auto|transparent|white|black|colour>`, `canvasExportColour`, `canvasExportVisibleOnly`, `canvasExportApplyMasks`, `canvasExportPrefix`, `canvasExportFiles`, `canvasExportSkipped`, `canvasExportEstimate`, `canvasExportError`, `canvasExportCancel`, `canvasExportOk`, the picker `canvasExportTarget`), and the Canvas view's File > Export rows `canvas-view.export-options` and `canvas-view.export-layers`.
- Old: the two rows were greyed placeholders; Export as… wrote by the file's extension only, at the canvas's size, with no say in quality.
- New: Export with quality and scale… (Save for Web): a Format row (PNG, JPEG, WebP, Layers), Quality (a `ValueSlider` for JPEG and a lossy WebP, a Lossless choice for WebP), Size (four percentage choices and a width and a height, which keep the proportions), Background, a 24u preview of the picture as encoded and, under, one line with its size in bytes and pixels, made again after each change. Export layers as files…: Format (PNG, WebP), two toggles (Visible layers only, Apply layer masks), the name prefix, the list of files with their sizes. Cancel, then the one highlighted Export… (an ellipsis: the desktop's file or folder picker follows).
- Why: the person chooses by looking (a preview and the size in bytes answer "is this quality enough" without a second try; Photoshop's Export As and Save for Web); every choice is one click on a visible row (Hick: four formats, four sizes, five backgrounds); the picker is the portal's (a learned place) and a file is never replaced.
- Rule: Floor (28px choices and fields, the slider is the existing 28px one). Primary (Export… is the only highlighted one; the choices use the quiet marking, D7). Destructive (none: a taken name refuses the whole export and says which). Gaps (1u inside a row of choices, 2u between buttons, 3u between rows). Posture: pointer first; Tab and Enter work (focus starts on Export…).
- Not done: the settings are not remembered between openings (Photoshop remembers); no way to set a width and height that break the proportions (use Canvas size…, #240).

### 2026-11-23 Canvas > Automate > Batch… (#250)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasBatchDialog.qml` (`canvasBatchDialog`, a `Question`; `canvasBatchTitle`, `canvasBatchAction-<id>`, `canvasBatchPrompt`, `canvasBatchChooseIn`, `canvasBatchInLabel`, `canvasBatchChooseOut`, `canvasBatchOutLabel`, `canvasBatchFormat-<png|jpg|webp|ora>`, `canvasBatchNote`, `canvasBatchSummary`, `canvasBatchResults`, `canvasBatchStatus`, `canvasBatchError`, `canvasBatchCancel`, `canvasBatchClose`, `canvasBatchAgain`, `canvasBatchRun`, the picker `canvasBatchFolderPicker`) and the row `canvas-view.batch` in the new **Automate** submenu of the Canvas view's Canvas menu (`menus.cpp`), in place of the greyed row "Run an action on a folder…".
- Old: the row was a greyed placeholder ("Not built yet (#250)").
- New: Batch… asks for an Action (a 28px choice per action that can run; one whose models are missing is greyed with a tip), a Prompt (only for an action that needs one), Pictures (**Choose folder…** and a line "12 pictures · /path"), Save to (the same) and a Format (PNG, JPEG, WebP, Layers). Cancel is **Hide** while it runs; the one highlighted answer is **Run**. After Run the same dialog shows a progress bar, a line ("1 of 3 pictures…" then the summary), a list of the pictures with Waiting, Running, Done, Failed (the reason under it in the error color) or Cancelled, **Cancel batch** while it runs and **Close** and **New batch** after.
- Why: Photoshop's Automate > Batch is the reference (an action, a source folder, a destination folder); each is one visible choice, in the order a person reads them (Hick: no step is hidden, the common case is PNG). The submenu is named for the family so the other Automate rows (droplets, scripts) have a place. The work runs in the background and the dialog may be hidden: a batch of fifty pictures on one GPU takes a long time, and the window stays free.
- Rule: Floor (28px choices and buttons; the results list rows are text). Primary (Run is the only highlighted one while setting up; Close once it ended; the choices use the quiet marking, D7). Destructive (none: nothing is replaced, a taken name gets -2, -3; Cancel batch asks nothing because it only stops what has not been written). Gaps (1u inside a row of choices, 2u between buttons, 3u between rows). Posture: pointer first; Tab and Enter work (focus starts on Run).
- Not done: a quality slider for JPEG and WebP (the batch writes 92; `fugue canvas batch --quality` sets it); Photoshop's other sources (Opened Files, Import) and its per-file naming options; recursion into subfolders; a selection or crop other than the whole picture (an action that needs a crop frame, Expand, is not offered); the settings are not remembered between openings.

### 2026-11-23 rulers, grid, guides and snap to them (#253, epic #216)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml` (`canvasViewOverlays`: `canvasViewGrid`, `canvasViewGuideLines`, `canvasViewRulerTop`, `canvasViewRulerLeft`; the options bar's `canvasViewGridSpacing`), `src/canvas/Ruler.qml` (`rulerPull`), `src/canvas/GridLines.qml` (`gridColumn`, `gridRow`), `src/canvas/GuideLines.qml` (`guide-<x|y>-<at>`, `guideGrab-<x|y>-<at>`, `guideDraft`). Commands `canvas-view.rulers`, `grid`, `guides`, `snap-to`, `clear-guides`; the View menu's last block.
- Old: one greyed row, "Rulers, grid and guides", with the tooltip "Not built yet (#253)".
- New: View > Rulers (Ctrl+R), Grid (Ctrl+'), Guides (Ctrl+;), Snap to guides and grid (Shift+Ctrl+;), Clear guides. Rulers are a 5u strip over the top and left edge of the picture, in canvas pixels, numbers on major ticks at least 56 view units apart; they follow zoom and pan. A drag from a ruler pulls out a guide (top: horizontal, left: vertical) with its position in a chip; a guide is dragged by a 12-unit strip along it and dropped on a ruler or off the picture to remove it. The Grid's spacing is a 30u `DragNumberField` in the options bar while the grid is on. Snap pulls the region's edges to a shown guide or grid line within 8 screen pixels.
- Why: a fixed canvas needs places to put things (Photoshop's View menu and keys, Blender's snapping, one keymap: D5). Snap works only on what is shown, so nothing invisible pulls (visibility of system status); dropping a guide on its ruler is the way it came out (direct manipulation, recognition over recall).
- Rules met: Floor (the guide's grab strip is 12 view units, a line is 1px; the spacing field is 28px; the rulers are not buttons, they are an edge), Primary (none: toggles in a menu), Destructive (a guide is removed by an explicit drop on the ruler or off the picture, and Clear guides is one row; guides are not part of the history, so neither is undoable: a guide is a few clicks to put back), Gaps (the spacing field sits in the options bar's Flow, 1u from its neighbours), Learned place (Photoshop's menu, keys and ruler gestures). Keys: Ctrl+R, Ctrl+', Ctrl+; and Ctrl+Shift+; had no owner in the registry.
- Not done: a New Guide… dialog (type a position); Lock guides; smart guides; grid style and color; the toggles are not remembered between sessions; snap now applies to a layer being moved or transformed (#236, `GuidesModel.snapFloating`: a layer may stick out of the canvas, which `snapRect` clamps); a Paste is not snapped (it is centered).

Commands `canvas-view.paste-layer` (Ctrl+V), `canvas-view.paste-canvas`, `canvas-view.place-picture`; the Edit menu's and the Canvas menu's rows.

- New: Edit > Paste as new layer (Ctrl+V), Edit > Paste as new canvas, Canvas > Place picture… and a picture file dropped on the picture. The layer lands above the current one, centered in what the view shows of the canvas (a drop: where it fell), named "Pasted 1"..., selected, ONE undoable step. A problem (no picture on the clipboard, too large, not a picture) is said in the line under the picture and nothing changes.

A drop accepts only picture files, so the window's own drop (a workflow, a Load Image) keeps the rest.

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml` (`copyPicture`, the rows' `menuState`, the `onWindowRequested` fallback); commands `canvas-view.copy` (Ctrl+C), `canvas-view.copy-merged` (Ctrl+Shift+C), `canvas-view.copy-mask` (no key); the Edit menu's three rows (`src/menu/menus.cpp`) in place of the greyed "Copy".

- New: Edit > Copy, Copy merged and Copy selection as mask. The clipboard gets the selected pixels (cut to the selection's bounds, the selection as alpha) or, for the mask, a gray picture of the canvas's size; a copy from a painted selection also carries the selection, which the Graph's paste keeps as the new Load Image's mask (one Ctrl+C, one Ctrl+V: Image and Mask). No dialog and no undo step (nothing in the canvas changes); the notice line under the picture says "Copied (120 x 80)"; a refusal is the error line under the picture.

A mask copy is one more row, not a key, since Photoshop has none and it is the rarer use.
- Rules met: Floor (rows of the shared menu), Primary (none: no new button), Destructive (none: nothing is changed or stored), Learned place (Edit menu, Photoshop's order: Copy, Copy Merged, Paste).

### 2026-11-23 Move tool and Free Transform (#236, epic #216)

- Control: `src/canvas/TransformFrame.qml` (`canvasViewTransformFrame`: `transformPreview`, `transformOutline`, `transformHandle-<side>` x8, `transformRotate-<uv>` x4, `transformBody`, `transformMoveAnywhere`), `CanvasView.qml` (tool strip `canvasViewTool-move`; options bar `canvasViewTransformX`, `Y`, `W`, `Link`, `H`, `Angle`, `canvasViewTransformFilter-<lanczos|bicubic|nearest>`, `canvasViewTransformApply`, `canvasViewTransformCancel`, `canvasViewMoveHint`). Commands `canvas-view.tool-move` (V), `canvas-view.free-transform` (Ctrl+T); Edit > Free transform, View > Tools > Move.
- Old: a greyed "Free transform" row (Edit), tooltip "Not built yet (#236)"; a layer could only be flipped or turned a quarter turn (Layer > Transform).
- New: a Move button in the tool strip (the 28px quiet-marked tool buttons; glyph `maximize-2`, the font has no `move` icon) and, for Free Transform, a box over the layer with eight 8px handles on 24px press targets, four turn rings outside the corners and the body to move; the options bar shows X, Y, W, Link, H, Angle (drag-number fields, 28px), the three resamplings (quiet marking) and Apply (the one highlighted button) and Cancel. Enter keeps, Escape leaves; arrows nudge 1 px, Shift 10.
- Why: Photoshop's Move (V) and Free Transform (Ctrl+T: handles, Shift for proportions and 15° turns, Alt from the center, Enter or Escape, numbers in the options bar) and Blender's transform gizmo (handles on the object, turn outside the corner). The same numbers by drag or by typing, so a person can be exact (Fitts: corners are the biggest targets; steering: a drag is computed from where it began). One revision per kept transform, so Undo is one step (D4).
- Rules met: Floor (24px press targets on the handles and rings, 28px fields), Primary (Apply is the only highlighted button; Cancel is quiet), Destructive (none: Undo, and the original is kept), Keys (one keymap with Photoshop; Enter and Escape act only while the box is open, the arrows only while the picture has the focus), Gaps (the chunks of the options bar are 1u apart, as the other tools').
- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): the Move tool moves the CURRENT layer wherever the press lands (no auto-select), and shows no handles (Ctrl+T does); a handle never flips the layer (Layer > Transform does); W and H are pixels, not percent; X and Y are the center; another tool picked while the box is open keeps the transform (as Photoshop does); the preview leaves out the layer mask and blend until it is kept; the Move glyph is `maximize-2` until a `move` glyph is added to the font subset.
- Not done: an auto-select of the layer under the pointer, a reference-point grid for X and Y, percent fields, skew and perspective and warp, transforming several layers or a group at once, a rotated cursor on the handles, a number chip near the pointer while dragging.

### 2026-11-23 cohesion, third batch: History retired, narrow areas, compare keys, shared rectangle footer (#228)

- Control: File > Canvas history… (`file.canvas-history`) and `CanvasHistoryDialog.qml` are gone (D1). `CanvasView.qml`: `sideWidth` (the History and Layers panels), `runTool` (`canvas-view.tool-region`, `canvas-view.mask-overlay` while Compare is on). New `RatioButtons.qml` (`cropRatio-<id>`, `regionRatio-<id>`, `cropRatioTurn`, `regionRatioTurn`) and `RectangleFields.qml` (`cropField-<id>`, `regionField-<id>`) in `CropDialog.qml` and `RegionDialog.qml` (C10). Spec 12 says "graph" for the node view (D10).
- Old: two windows for one job, with a Compare that meant two things; the History and Layers panels were 56u each however narrow the area; M and `\` did nothing in the view's compare; Crop and Region each copied the ratio row and the four fields.
- New: the view does what History did: the History panel lists the revisions, **All revisions** the kept-apart ones too, File > Open canvas… the canvases, Export and Compare are the view's own. With History gone C5 (one Export button and file dialog) has nothing left to share. A panel is 56u wide in a roomy view and a quarter of a narrow one, never under 32u (a 400px area gave half its width to the layers). While Compare is on, M steps through the modes and `\` flips A and B (the keys of Compare Images, `compare.next-mode`, `compare.flip`); they keep their Photoshop jobs (Rectangle region, Overlay) otherwise, as the picture has no region or overlay while comparing. The ratio row and the X, Y, Width, Height fields are two components both windows use; the ratio is the quiet marking, OK the one accent fill (D7).
- Why: one place to look at a revision, one meaning of Compare (D1); a view that is a small area stays usable (Fitts: the picture is the target); the same keys for the same job (D5); one component per repeated control (D4).
- Rules met: Floor (no control resized), Primary (unchanged: Crop and Region's OK), Words (one name for the work: History panel), Grouping (the two components keep the order X, Y, Width, Height and the ratios).
- Checked: a disabled `ViewButton` gets no hover in Qt (a test hovered a disabled Redo: `hovered` stayed false), so a tooltip that says why a button is off is never seen. Left: a button's reason for being off has to be a visible line or its own enabled tip, not the tooltip (the Canvas Region tip at `CanvasView.qml` "Add or select a Canvas Region node first" and the batch dialog's missing-models tip are affected); a fix needs a hover target above the button. Fixed in #281: while disabled, `ViewButton` (and the Media view's bar button) watches the pointer through a non-blocking `HoverHandler` on its parent, in the parent's coordinates, so every call site's tip shows unchanged and the click stays blocked (the button's own handlers are as disabled as the button). Window tests: Use selection with no Canvas Region (`test_canvas_tools_window`) and the batch dialog's missing-models action (`test_canvas_batch_runs`) show their tips on hover.

### 2026-11-23 Lasso and a layer as the selection (#230, #231 slice 3, epic #216)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml`: the strip button `canvasViewTool-lasso` (after Rectangle region, 4u chunks kept), the selection over the picture `canvasViewSelection` (now also shown while Quick Mask is off and a selection exists); the pointer is `BrushPen.qml` with `selects` on. `src/canvas/CanvasLayersPanel.qml`: Ctrl+click on `canvasLayerMask-<n>`, and a "Selection" group in a layer's row menu (Selection from layer, Selection from layer mask). Command `canvas-view.tool-lasso` (L; spec 12.4). Glyph `lasso-select` (already in the subset).
- Old: the selection was a rectangle or a Quick Mask painted with the brush, and it counted for Generate only while Quick Mask was on; a layer mask could not become a selection.
- New: Lasso (L): a freehand drag draws a closed outline that is a selection when the button is let go (anti-aliased edge); no key makes a new selection (it replaces), Shift adds, Alt subtracts (Photoshop's modifiers); Escape drops a drag. The selection is the mask Quick Mask paints, shown as the same red tint whether or not Quick Mask is on; a drawn rectangle replaces it, and the task bar uses it while it exists. Ctrl+click a layer mask's thumbnail makes it the selection, with Shift added, with Alt subtracted; the row menu does it without the keys. Select > Inverse, Deselect and Modify work on it. Ctrl+Z over the view takes back the selection step while the lasso is the tool, or right after a selection changed (a new revision ends that); else it is the canvas's.
- Why: Photoshop's L and its load-selection gesture (References); one mask store for every kind of selection so Generate, Modify and the Region see one thing (D1); modifiers over modes, as the brush's Alt already is (D5).
- Rules met: Floor (the strip button is 28px), Gaps (1u in the chunk), Learned place (the tool strip order kept, Lasso beside the Rectangle: both make a selection), Keys: L checked against the registry: only `view.toggle-links` (context `canvas`, the Graph) owns it, and `canvas-view` wins while the pointer is over the view; Ctrl+click on the thumbnail was unused (Ctrl+click on the row toggles the layer's selection, the thumbnail is the thumbnail's own press, as Shift+click is).
- Deviations, each to confirm: marching ants came with #313 (below), so the red tint is now Quick Mask's only; Enter does not close the outline, the release does (a lasso is one drag); the modifiers are read at the press or the release; there is no polygonal (click-to-click) lasso; Selection from layer is the layer's alpha, from the row menu only.
- Not seen in a real window.

### 2026-11-23 Questions keep their frame inside a zero-size host (fix)

- Control: `Question.qml` and the three questions that set their own width (`CanvasBatchDialog`, `CanvasExportDialog`, `NewCanvasDialog`); every `Question` declared inside `CanvasFileDialogs` (Rename, Share, Clear, Delete canvas, Layer properties), `CanvasSelectionDialogs`, `GroupDialogs` and other hosts that are a zero-size `Item`.
- Old: a question's width was the parent's width less 12u. A question declared in a host `Item` of size 0 got a negative width, so the surface had no frame, the text sat at the popup's edge and the answers touched it (the Delete canvas… question, reported with a screenshot).
- New: the width comes from the window's overlay (the item the question is centred in), at most 520px (560 and 640 for the Batch, New canvas and Export dialogs): the surface, the 6u padding and the 4u gap under the text hold wherever the question is declared. `tests/canvas/test_canvas_view_window.cpp` (`aQuestionWithFilledContentKeepsItsPaddingAndGaps`) guards the margins (2u inside the surface, the answers at least 2u under the text).
- Why: a popup's parent is where it is declared, not where it shows; sizing from it made the look depend on the host.

### 2026-11-23 Marching ants (#313, from #232)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml`: `canvasViewAnts` (`SelectionAnts`, `src/canvas/selection_ants.{h,cpp}`, the outline `selection_contour.{h,cpp}`), between the region frame and the grid and guides; `canvasViewSelection` (the red tint) is shown only while Quick Mask is on (since 2026-11-23 below: optional, off to start).
- Old: a rectangle was the white frame; a painted, lasso or layer-mask selection showed as the red tint whether or not Quick Mask was on, so the picture stayed red until Deselect.
- New: the selection's edge is an animated black and white dashed line (Photoshop's marching ants): the rectangle's four sides, and the contour of the painted, lasso or layer-mask selection (the mask at 50%, so a feathered edge is outlined in its middle). The dashes move 8 px a second, redrawn 8 times a second, and nothing is redrawn while there is no selection. They follow pan and zoom, hide while Quick Mask edits the selection (the red tint is its editing view) and while a rectangle is dragged, and Select > Deselect (Ctrl+D, Escape) removes them. Each ant is 4 px of black then 4 px of white on a 1px white line, so it reads on any picture; the rectangle's handles stay on top of it.
- Why: Photoshop's selection marking is marching ants and its Quick Mask the editing view (References); the owner asked for it on #232. One look for every kind of selection (D1); a tint over a finished selection hid the picture being judged.
- Rules met: Floor and Gaps (no control added or moved), Keys (none added), Words (no text). Cheap: the contour is marching squares over the part of the picture in view only, on a grid of 2 screen pixels (a power-of-two step in picture pixels, aligned so it does not shimmer when panned), cached until the mask, the zoom or the view changes; a 16384 px canvas costs what a 1000 px one does at the same zoom.
- Deviations, each to confirm: the edge of a mask selection is within one grid cell (at most 1 screen px) of the true pixel edge, not exact; no reduced-motion setting exists, so the ants always move; the item is a CPU-painted item, so each of the 8 redraws re-uploads the view-sized texture (a custom scene-graph node would be cheaper, not needed yet); the ants are above the region frame's line but under the grid and guides; "a brush release becomes the selection" is not done (see canvas.md).
- Not seen in a real window (offscreen stills: tokyo-night, rectangle and lasso).

### 2026-11-23 A brush release becomes the selection (#313)

- Control: `src/canvas/CanvasView.qml`: `canvasViewAnts` (now shown with Quick Mask on too), `canvasViewSelection` (the tint: `selectionOverlay`), the options bar's Overlay button (`canvasViewMaskOverlay`, now also in Quick Mask, its tip "Show the selection in red (\)"), `BrushPen` `onPressedAt`/`onPainted`.
- Old: in Quick Mask a stroke only painted the red tint; the selection showed as marching ants only after leaving Q. Modify and Transform switched Q on, hiding the ants they had just changed. Use selection, Select all and Ctrl+Z after Deselect needed Quick Mask on.
- New: a stroke is the selection when the button is let go: the ants outline the painted mask at once and follow the stroke as it grows, Q on or off. A stroke adds (brush) or subtracts (eraser, X swaps); the first stroke on a rectangle paints the rectangle into the mask first. The red tint is optional (\ in Quick Mask, off to start, separate from a layer mask's Overlay) and shows by itself when the ants have nothing to outline (a stroke under 50% opacity). Each stroke is one step of the selection's undo; Ctrl+Z works after leaving Q and after Deselect. Modify and Transform leave Q as it was. Select all fills the painted selection when one exists; Use selection works whenever a painted selection exists. The ants draw the rectangle only when no painted selection exists.
- Why: Photoshop's Quick Selection brush and Quick Mask end in marching ants (References); the owner decided on #313 that the release is the selection. Add, never replace: a brush cannot cover an area in one stroke, so replacing on release would wipe each earlier stroke (lasso-then-brush refining stays). Q stays because with Q off the brush paints the layer mask.
- Rules met: Floor and Gaps (no control added or moved; Overlay now also shows in Quick Mask), Keys (\ reused in Quick Mask, checked against the registry: `canvas-view.mask-overlay`), Words (one tip).
- Deviations, each to confirm: the Graph's Canvas Region node is written only by Use selection, not by a stroke (a PNG and an undo step per stroke); the selection does not limit where a layer mask is painted (Photoshop's does; its own issue); the tint is off to start, where a layer mask's Overlay is on; no reduced-motion setting exists.
- Not seen in a real window (offscreen still: brush-selection.png).

### 2026-11-26 favorites, trash, re-run, send, zoom and compare in the media browser (#133)

- Controls: `MediaBrowser.qml` bar `mediaFit`, `mediaActualSize`, `mediaCompare`, `mediaCompareModes` (`mediaCompareMode-<mode>`), `mediaCompareHint`; the actions row `mediaAction-favorite|open|rerun|workflow|send|folder|copy|delete`; the question `mediaConfirm` (`mediaConfirmYes`, `mediaConfirmNo`) that replaces the actions row; `WorkflowsPanel.qml` `workflowsResultsFavorites`; a star (`favoriteMark`) on a favorite's cell in `MediaThumb.qml`; `MediaSingle.qml` now shows an image in `CompareView`.
- Old: three actions (Open, Show folder, Copy path), the viewer a fitted picture, the switch row a fixed Row, the mode and view forgotten.
- New: the actions row wraps and has eight, Favorite first (a toggle, F), the destructive one last and red; Delete asks "Move <file> to the trash?" in the same place with the red confirming button first and Cancel after, and Escape cancels; Fit and 100% (0 and 1) and the wheel and a drag zoom and pan an image; Compare pins A and shows A and B in one frame; the switch row is a Flow so Favorites does not push the panel's narrow width.
- Why: favorite and delete are the first things done to a result (Hick: the frequent actions come first, the rare and destructive last, apart by the wrap); a deletion is confirmed where the eye already is, not in a dialog (Fitts: the pointer is on the actions row), and it goes to the trash so it can be undone; zoom and compare use the same viewer, keys and wheel as the Canvas view and Compare Images (Learned place).
- Rule: Floor (every button is a `ViewButton`, 28px). Primary: none (browsing has none; the confirming button in the question is the one red action). Destructive: Move to trash is the last action, `danger`, never the default (Enter does not confirm; focus returns to the browser after a press so Delete and the arrows still work). Gaps (1u in a row; the question stands where the actions did). Posture: a docked panel, so the canvas's density.
- Not checked by a person: the panel at its narrowest width (the rows wrap to more lines), the star in a light theme.

### 2026-11-24 Templates window: themed drop-downs, a search in their lists, a details view

- Control: `src/workflow/TemplatesDialog.qml` (now up to 1200 x 800 within the window less 12u; cards keep 56u), its search field, the Source and Model family drop-downs (`templatesSource`, `templatesFamily`), and a details sheet over the grid, `src/workflow/TemplateDetails.qml` (`templateDetails`, with `templateDetailsOpen`, `templateDetailsCancel`). New reusable control `src/canvas/ThemedDropDown.qml`: a 7u well with a hairline edge (the accent edge while focused or open), the current text, a chevron, and the list in `WidgetMenu` (the model picker's list), with an optional search field (`searchable`, word-start matching: "ima" finds "Z-Image", "mage" does not). The model picker's "Family: ..." menu and Settings > Models' family filter use the same list, with the same search.
- Old: two Material ComboBoxes 56px high, cut off at the names, a white focus edge; a search field as tall; a 960 x 640 window; a click on a card opened the template at once, the description cut at two lines.
- New: the search field, the drop-downs and the Ready to run chip are 7u (28px) in one row, in the settings fields' look; a drop-down is as wide as its text up to 48u, then elides with the full text in a tooltip, and its list is as wide as its longest entry; an entry that would leave no template (counting the other choices) is dimmed. A click on a card, or Enter on it, opens it at once; the small round **i** chip at the picture's bottom right corner (`templateInfo-<id>`, the Lucide `info` glyph on a translucent disc, dark on white where the picture's corner is light and white on black where it is dark, chosen from the corner's mean luminance against 0.6 (`workflow/corner_brightness`, cached, `lightCorner` in the list), 55% visible, fully on card hover or focus, tooltip "About this template"), or I or Space on the focused card, shows the details; Escape closes the details (the card has the focus again), then the dialog. The details: the preview cropped wide with a fade, the title, the whole description (the sheet scrolls), the source line with its link, category, families, tags, each model (type, size, license when the catalog knows the file; on disk, served or missing), the packs needed, the sample inputs, Cancel and Open (primary). Cancel returns to the grid with the same card focused; Open does what opening a card did, including the Get ready sheet.
- Why: details before the open is the Photoshop (New from template) and Blender (splash and File > New) convention: look, then commit; a card's two-line description could not say what a template does or needs. One drop-down for every choice list, so the Templates window, the model picker and Settings look and behave the same.
- Rule: Floor (28px controls), Primary (Open on the details; none in the grid), Learned place (a click and Enter open a card as before the sheet existed; Escape backs out one layer).
- 2026-11-24 (owner, from the real window): the click-shows-details decision above is replaced. Clicking a card opens the template as before the details sheet; an **i** info button opens the sheet (Lucide `info`, added to `lucide-fugue`). The 400 ms double-click passthrough is gone. Why: the owner wanted the common action, open, one click away, with the details an opt-in. Later the same day the owner found the faint outlined chip hard to see: it is now the place badge's chip (theme body, strong hairline, 16px + 1u high) in the opposite corner, always fully shown, with a bold serif "i" that turns accent on hover; the corner-brightness rule went with it, since a solid chip reads on any picture.
- 2026-11-24 (UI review of the whole window, from renders dark and light, wide and 760px): ranked findings and what changed. High: (1) card text at 10px (`captionSize`) was too small, and the description, a source line and the status were cut with "..." in the middle of words; the description now uses `labelSize` (11) and takes all the room the card has (2 to 4 lines, the whole text on hover when cut), the title wraps to two lines (whole title on hover when cut), the status wraps to two lines. (2) Uneven card heights: the optional "Based on..." line made gaps; the line left the card (it is on the details sheet, `templateDetailsSource`), the card has one fixed anatomy (picture, title, description filling the middle, foot pinned to the bottom) and every card is the same height, computed from the font metrics. (3) The foot: "Models missing: 3 of 3" and "Download 19.3 GB" did not fit one line in a 56u card; they are now two lines, the status above and the download as a bordered button (a flat one read as text). (4) Narrow window: the search shrank to a few letters; the filter row is a Flow, the search takes what the filters leave and the filters wrap under it when under 28u would remain; under 220u of dialog width the category list is 32u instead of 44u. Medium: (5) focus rings on cards, categories and the Ready to run chip are 2px (the button's ring), not 1px; (6) the info chip is a 20px visual with a 28px press target (4px beyond it, Floor); (7) the footer note is `labelSize`; (8) the details sheet text column is at most 150u wide and centred (190 characters per line before) with the banner kept full width.
- Recommended, not done: counts beside the category names (as the drop-downs have), a "Ready to run" status line on ready cards (needs the model's `status` for ready, which tests read as empty), Open folder on the footer note's path, and a larger banner source in the details (the preview is a 512px thumbnail stretched wide, so it looks soft). The info chip (solid chip, bold serif "i") was reviewed: it reads on bright and dark pictures in both themes and matches the place chip; kept. Pictures: `FUGUE_SHOT_DIR=<dir> QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen build/test_readme_shots templatesDialog templatesDetails templatesLayouts` (`templates-light.png`, `templates-narrow.png`, `templates-narrow-light.png` beside the details ones).
- Not done: the Recommended mark (#370) on a model row of the details.

### 2026-11-25 App view: takes from History, Live and Repeat (#389, #471)

- Controls: the takes strip (`appTakes`, `AppTakes.qml`) and, at the input panel's foot above Run, one KitRow "Run again" with a KitSegmented (`appLiveMode`): **Off**, **On change** (Live: run again when an input changes) and **Repeat** (run again when a run ends). One three-way mode is one chooser (coordinator, 2026-11-25: two switches read as two settings and cost a row of the form).
- Old: the strip held this session's runs only; no way to re-run on change.

- Keys: Tab reaches both switches (after Advanced, before Run), Space or Enter flips one (a click anywhere on the row too); in the strip Left and Right move, Enter or Space shows, **R** runs again with the take's inputs (bare R is free there: the strip is a list, not a text field, and no command owns the bare letter).

- Rules met: Floor (the kit's 7u switch in a 10u row), Primary (Run alone is the accent), Gaps (the group is the panel's padding from its sides and Run), Learned place (above Run, with the other per-app state; the canvas's Live is in its task bar), Keys (R and the switches reachable by Tab; checked against the registry: no bare-letter command).

### 2026-11-25 App view as a view type of an area (#450, epic #397)

- Control: `src/apps/AppArea.qml` (`appArea`, `appAreaNote`) holding `AppPage.qml`, registered as the `app` view type (`workspace/view_registry.cpp`, one per window, 800 x 360 px minimum); the header's type menu lists App beside Graph and Canvas (the Dusk tone, as the Graph's); `view.toggle-app` and the App | Graph switch work through the area's view type (`Workspace::toggleApp`).

In a split the active area decides what Ctrl+Enter, Ctrl+V and the bare keys act on. The page exists only while an area shows the App; an area showing it over a workflow with no app block says "This workflow is not an app.".

- Rules met: Floor (the area header's controls as every area's), Primary (Run stays the App view's one accent), Destructive (none), Keys (Ctrl+Alt+A unchanged; a press in an area or Tab into it makes it active; Tab inside the page as in the one-area window), Learned place (the type menu and the window bar's switch, no new control), Right-to-left (the areas mirror as before).

- Decisions taken without the owner (each to confirm): the App is `one-per-window` (one store, one form per tab), high; in a split the active area decides `AppView.shown`, so keys follow the area the person works in, medium-high; splitting from the one-area App view does not carry the App into the split, medium; no new `area.view-app` command (view.toggle-app has the key and the menus' rows), medium-high.

The trust questions (this page's, the Get ready sheet's, the workflow's) get one more line, "Reads secrets: token_1, token_2"; this page's and the CLI's add "Already set" / "set for another source" and say that isolation between packs is advisory.
- Old: a pack had nowhere to keep a key; only providers did.
- New: type the value and press Enter (or click Set…); the field empties and the state says "set"; the value is never shown again. Clear drops it at once (it is quick and undoes by typing it again, so no question).
- Rules met: Keyboard (Tab to the field, Enter sets; Clear and Set… are buttons in the same tab order), Floor (the field is the settings' `Field`, the buttons the settings' `SmallButton`), Primary (no accent on the row: setting a key is not the page's main action), Learned place (the same field, placeholder and "set" wording as the Providers page's key).
